   3| PREAMBLE
   4| The Lunenburg Town Center region has tremendou s importance in symbolizing the unique character and heritage of
   5| Lunenburg.  As residents engage with the historic buildings and streetscape of this area in the course of their routine daily
   6| business, be it school related, recreational, commercial, or even  just driving through, the experience greatly reinforces a
   7| profound bond with the town’s present community and past heritage.
   8| There is an opportunity to significantly improve the quality of this already poignant civic experience and increase the numbe r
   9| of people who will be drawn to share in it by expanding the businesses and other attractions in the area.  There is also a key
  10| challenge to preserving this critical embodiment of our town. Appropriate new uses or remedies must be found to the
  11| increasing number of underutilized buildings located about the town center.
  12| Town planning experience and best practices, as exemplified in the state’s model bylaw for Village Center Districts,
  13| demonstrates some key ingredients to sustaining and enriching this type of  environment in small towns.  One of these is
  14| careful stewardship through clear guidelines for development consistent with what the area means to the community.
  15| Another is encouraging a level of intensive mixed use development, so that the critical mass o f social and economic activity
  16| can be reached that will naturally provide the resources and inspiration required to maintain and organically adapt the area
  17| landmarks, buildings, and infrastructure without excessive burden on the Town’s public finances.
  18| 4.17.1. Purpose
  19| In pursuit of this opportunity and to address this challenge, the Town implements this bylaw and designates this zoning
  20| district as Village Center District in order to encourage economic and residential growth that fits the character of the To wn.
  21| The purposes of the Village Center District are to:
  22| a. Build upon the historic development patterns in the existing village centers to create attractive, walkable neighborhoods;
  23| b. Encourage adaptive reuse of abandoned, vacant or underutilized buildings or structures where appropriate;
  24| c. Allow for a mix of new land uses that are appropriate to both the needs of the community and the scale of surrounding
  25| neighborhoods;
  26| d. Provide incentives to develop larger parcels at higher densities and in a coordina ted, planned approach;
  27| e. Maintain a consistently high level of design quality throughout the district;
  28| f. Encourage the development of mixed use buildings and campuses that are designed and constructed in a manner that is
  29| contextually sensitive to the existing structures and facilities.
  30| 4.17.2. Establishment
  31| The Village Center District is hereby established as of 1 January 2016 and consists of the area shown as the Village Center
  32| District on Town of Lunenburg Zoning Map on file with the Town/City Clerk an d dated 2 May 2015 as may be amended
  33| from time to time by Town Meeting.
  34| 4.17.3. Definitions
  35| Artist Space: A shop for the creation and/or sale of goods made by a potter, ceramicist, silversmith, jewelry maker, painter,
  36| photographer or other fine artist. Th is space may also serve as a living space for the artist provided there is adequate
  37| separation between uses per the governing Building and Health Codes.
  38| Banner: A strip of cloth or other man made fabric on which a sign is painted, silk -screened or printed. The display area shall
  39| be defined as the area of one face of the banner. The banner shall not exceed (40) square feet and shall not be displayed for
  40| more than fourteen (14) consecutive days.
  41| Bed & Breakfast:  Bed and Breakfast, or Bed and Breakfast Establishment provided that:
  42| a) The dwelling is owner occupied.

===PAGE 161=== (plain, 46 lines)
   1| 161
   2| b) The lot conforms to all dimensional requirements of the Zoning Bylaw.
   3| c) Parking for each room available for rent is provided in the side yard behind the setback line or in the rear yard, but no t
   4| nearer than ten (10) feet to any property line. One (1) parking place shall be provided for each bedroom.
   5| d) The outside appearance of the building is not altered from that of a residence, except to comply with the Building Code.
   6| Civic Use: A land use th at provides a public, cultural, or institutional benefit to the community. Specific uses may include,
   7| but shall not be limited to, government offices, religious institutions, educational institutions, and medical facilities (no t
   8| including veterinary operations). For the purposes of this bylaw, civic uses shall not include open space as defined below.
   9| Multiple Commercial Use : A combination of commercial uses within a single building or lot.  In the case of uses that fall
  10| under the Special Permit requirements  of Section 4.17.5.b. a Zoning Board of Appeals decision must be rendered on the use
  11| prior to the filing for Development Plan Review.
  12| Mixed Use: A combination of commercial and residential uses within a single building or lot. In the case of uses that fall
  13| under the Special Permit requirements of Section 4.17.5.b. a Zoning Board of Appeals decision must be rendered on the use
  14| prior to the filing for Development Plan Review.
  15| Personal Service: An establishment engaged in the provision of services to the gene ral public which are conducted within the
  16| structure. This includes, but is not limited to, barbers, hair salons, massage therapists, chiropractors and nail salons.
  17| Professional Office: An office primarily devoted to professional activities, including, but not limited to, banks, real estate,
  18| insurance or other agency offices; an office of an accountant, physician, teacher, dentist, engineer, lawyer, homebuilder,
  19| photographer, or other recognized professional for which specific training and/or licensing is re quired. Drive -thru service
  20| shall be limited to banks and shall require a Special Permit.
  21| Open Space: In the context of an application for a Village Center District Special Permit, this term denotes open areas set
  22| aside for public use as part of a coordinat ed site development process. Specific requirements for ownership and maintenance
  23| are provided in Section 4.17.9. and shall not include areas covered with impervious surface treatments.
  24| Transparency: The amount of transparent space that occupies a building façade including standard street -level windows and
  25| doorway windows.
  26| Restaurant: A place serving food and beverage within a structure or patio, primarily for consumption on the premises. Drive -
  27| thru windows shall be prohibited for this use.
  28| Retail: An establishment engaged in displaying and selling goods or merchandise within a building to the general public or
  29| to business establishments where the goods or merchandise are not intended for resale. There shall be no outdoor display of
  30| goods without the granting of Special Permit from the Board of Appeals.
  31| 4.17.4. Authority
  32| The Planning Board shall act as the administering authority for any Development Plan Review procedure associated with this
  33| bylaw Section 8.4. The Board of Appeals shall serve as the Special P ermit Granting Authority for any use that requires a
  34| Special Permit in the district, any use requiring a Special Permit pursuant to Section 4.17.5.
  35| 4.17.5. Use Provisions
  36| a. The following uses are allowed by-right subject to any Development Plan Review requirements listed in Section 8.4 and all
  37| applicable density and design provisions listed in this bylaw.
  38| 1) Retail, with maximum 5,000 sq. ft. of floor area
  39| 2) Professional Office Space
  40| 3) Personal Service
  41| 4) Artist space
  42| 5) Restaurant
  43| 6) Bed & Breakfast
  44| 7) Mixed Use containing less than  5,000 sq. ft. per individual proposed use
  45| 8) Multiple Commercial Use containing less than 5,000 sq. ft.  per individual proposed use
  46| 9) Civic Uses

===PAGE 162=== (plain, 44 lines)
   1| 162
   2| b. The following uses are allowed only through the granting of a Special Permit by the Board of Appeals  pursuant to the
   3| procedures outlined in Section 8.3.
   4| 1) Single and Two-Family Homes
   5| 2) Multi-Family Homes
   6| 3) Apartment Complexes
   7| 4) Movie House/ Theatre (maximum of two screens)
   8| 5) Liquor Stores
   9| 6) Outdoor Markets subject to applicable licensing requirements
  10| 7) Private Club
  11| 8) Drive Through Use for Banks
  12| 9) Retail Use greater than 5,000 sq. ft.
  13| 10) Uses allowed by -right with a floor area greater than 5,000 sq. ft. included in a Multiple Commercial Use or Mixed
  14| Use development.
  15| c. Special Permit Approval Criteria – The Zoning Board of A ppeals shall act upon all applications a fter notice and public
  16| hearing, and after due consideration may grant such a special permit provided that it finds the proposed use:
  17| a. Is in harmony with the purpose and intent of the Bylaw and will promote the purp oses of the Village Center District.
  18| b. The use is in harmony with those of the abutting properties and the Village Center District as a whole.
  19| c. All applicable standards for use, parking, and dimensional requirements are met;
  20| d. All applicable design standards listed in Section 8 are met.
  21| e. Where multiple structures are proposed, the site design reflects a thoughtful arrangement of elements that will
  22| facilitate the movement of pedestrians between structures through the use of sidewalks, internal walkway s, alleys or
  23| open space features.
  24| d. The following uses are prohibited in the Village Center District
  25| 1) Newly constructed one-story buildings on lots of less than 5 acres.
  26| 2) Retail operations with more than five thousand (5,000) square feet of gross flo or area on any individual floor, except
  27| for reuse of buildings existing prior to 1 May 2015.
  28| 3) Drive through establishments, excluding those expressly allowed by Special Permit.
  29| 4.17.6. Dimensional Requirements
  30| a.
  31| Building Type
  32| Mixed Use with
  33| Commercial 1st
  34| Floor
  35| Other Mixed-Use
  36| or Residential Only
  37| Maximum Floor Area Ratio (FAR)
  38| (Gross Floor Area/Lot Size)
  39| 2.0 1.5
  40| Minimum Lot Frontage 75 feet
  41| Maximum Lot Coverage 85%
  42| Minimum Lot Area N/A
  43| Number of Buildings Per Lot See Section 4.17.9
  44| Maximum Building Frontage 300 feet

===PAGE 163=== (plain, 33 lines)
   1| 163
   2| Minimum Front Setback  5 feet
   3| Maximum Front Setback w/o street furniture 15 feet
   4| Minimum Side/Rear Setback abutting a Residential
   5| Zone
   6| 10 feet
   7| Minimum Side/Rear Setback in VCD 5 feet
   8| Interior Setback (Between Buildings on the Same
   9| Lot)
  10| 10 feet
  11| b. Height Limitations for Non -residential and Mixed Use: Building height for mixed use or non -residential use shall not
  12| exceed thirty-eight (38) feet and no building shall have more than three (3) stories.
  13| 4.17.7. Parking Requirements
  14| Use Parking Requirement
  15| Retail/Personal Service/Liquor Store  1 parking space per 400 sq. ft. of net floor area, plus 1
  16| space per employee on the largest shift
  17| Professional Office/Civic Uses 1 parking space per 300 sq. ft. of net floor area
  18| Artists Space 1 parking space per 400 sq. ft. of net retail/gallery floor
  19| area, plus 2 parking space per dwelling unit
  20| Restaurant/Private Club 1 parking space per 4 seats, plus one for each employee
  21| on the largest shift
  22| Bed & Breakfast 1 parking space per accommodation room, plus 2
  23| parking spaces for the primary residence, plus 1 per non-
  24| resident employee on the largest shift.
  25| Mixed Use/Multiple Commercial Parking to be Provided per the Individual Uses
  26| All Residential Uses 2 parking spaces per dwelling unit
  27| Movie House 1 parking space per 4 occupants, plus 1 parking space
  28| per 2 employees on the largest shift
  29| Outdoor Market 1 parking space per 500 sq. ft.
  30| As part of a Development Plan approval or Special Permit process within this district, the applicant may req uest reductions to
  31| minimum requirements or alternative methods for meeting the required parking. Available innovative parking strategies
  32| include:
  33| a. Shared On-Site Parking

===PAGE 164=== (plain, 51 lines)
   1| 164
   2| 1) Non-competing Uses. In mixed -use developments, applicants may propose a reduction  in parking requirements
   3| based on an analysis of peak demands for noncompeting uses. Up to 50% of the requirements for the predominant
   4| use may be waived by the Planning Board or the Board of Appeals if the applicant can demonstrate that the peak
   5| demands fo r two uses do not overlap. An additional 25% may be waived with the addition of bicycle parking
   6| facilities.
   7| b. Off-Site Parking
   8| Separate from, or in conjunction with Shared Parking provisions, an applicant may use off -site parking to satisfy their
   9| parking requirements in accordance with the following conditions:
  10| 1) Off-site parking shall be within one-thousand (1000) feet of the property for which it is being requested.
  11| 2) Off -site parking spaces provided by a separate private property owner shall be subje ct to a legally binding
  12| agreement that will be presented to the Permit Granting Authority as a condition of either the Development Plan
  13| Review or the Special Permit. Where an agreement shall expire within a specified timeline, the applicant or current
  14| property owner shall continue to provide evidence to the Zoning Enforcement Agent that the agreement has been
  15| extended. The Permit Granting Authority will verify with the Zoning Enforcement Officer that there are no existing
  16| parking agreements during the permitting process.
  17| 4.17.8. Design Standards
  18| The Design Standards in this section shall be applied to development within the Village Center District where applicable.
  19| a. Occupied Lot Area.
  20| 1) The total lot devoted to building, parking, outdoor storage and display an d other impervious surfaces may
  21| occupy up to eighty-five (85%) percent of the total lot area.
  22| b. Buildings
  23|      1) All buildings shall have a principal façade and entry (with operable doors) facing a stree t or open space. Buildings
  24|          may have more than one principal façade and/or entry.
  25|       2) Building finish materials shall be appropriate to traditional New England architecture.
  26|       3) Building facades, materials and roof lines shall be reviewed under the permit granting autho rity to ensure consistency
  27|            and compatibility with other structures.
  28|       4) Blank walls adjacent to streets, alleys or open spaces shall not be permitted. Where windows are not possible or
  29|            appropriate to the intended use, vertical articulation in the form of raised or recessed surfaces shall be used to break up
  30|            blank walls.
  31|       5) New retail buildings shall have one of the following features along the front entrances to pedestrians: awning,
  32|             marquee, arcade and/ or colonnade.
  33|       6) Flat roofs that are visible from the street are prohibited unless an appropriate façade is included in the design.
  34|       7) Larger buildings with multiple non-residential tenants on the first floor shall articulate the façade in a manner that
  35|           distinguishes the location of these tenants through the use of decorative raised or depressed ve rtical surfaces, variations
  36|           of acceptable signage, awnings, marquees, colonnades or arcades.
  37|       8) Mixed Use buildings shall have no more than twenty five (25%) percent of the first floor dedicated to residential use
  38|           Conversion or partial conversion of residential uses existing before 1 May 2015 are exempt from this provision.
  39|       9) All new utility service connections shall be placed underground.
  40|      10) Lighting, signage and architectural style shall be consistent with other uses in the District and reviewed under the
  41|              Permit Granting Authority.
  42| c. Signs
  43| 1) Primary signs shall be flat against the façade, or mounted projecting from the façade.
  44| 2) Signs that project from buildings shall have at least ten (10) feet of clearance from the ground level.
  45| 3) Signs shall be externally lit from the front; lighting shall be provided using a gooseneck fixture . Back lighting of signs
  46| shall not be used.
  47| 4) Neon, flashing signs, moving signs, electric message signs, and roof signs shall not be used, except for barber poles.
  48| 5) Banners with a specific date of expiration, shall be allowed, after approval by the Zoning En forcement Officer.
  49| 6) Signs shall be made of attractive materials consistent with the character of the district. Materials may include wood
  50| (painted or natural), stone, copper, brass, galvanized steel, painted canvas or paint/engraved on façade surface.
  51| 7) Signs may only be incorporated into the skirt of awnings and not on the primary angled surface.

===PAGE 165=== (plain, 50 lines)
   1| 165
   2| 8) Each building façade facing a street or parking area is permitted to have signs that equal a maximum square footage of five
   3| (5%) percent of the respective façade.
   4| Removed 9) A building with multiple businesses shall have signs of equal size on the façade, signs shall be either
   5| rectangular or oval shaped.
   6| Removed 10) Free standing signs shall be prohibited except for municipal use, portable and temporary sign s.
   7| Renumbered 9) Sandwich board signs, flags and other portable signs are permitted only within the property lines, as long as
   8| they are properly weighted.
   9| Renumbered 10) Window signs, comprising no more than ten (10%) percent of the transparent surface, shall be allowed.
  10| Window signs shall not be lighted or animated in any manner, with the exception of the standard lighting fixtures within the
  11| building.
  12| d. Site Design
  13| 1)  Interior Streets, Drives, Walkways and Access.
  14|                   a) Site access shall be a single divided way (one way in and one way out) where and when appropriate as
  15|                       determined by the Permit Granting Authority.
  16|                   b) Surfaces shall be pervious when possible and practical.
  17|                   c) Street level frontage shall be devoted to entrances, shop windows or other displays.
  18|                   d) Primary entrances to proposed and existing buildings shall be situated on pedestrian amenities (e.g., sidewalks,
  19|                    plazas or open space) with a minimum width of 10 feet.
  20|                 e) Setbacks shall be consistent with the fabric of the existing street and do not preclude pedestrian access.
  21| 2) Parking and Loading Area.
  22|                    a) Parking shall be in the rear or side of building(s) and shall not be visible from the street line when possible.
  23|                        Parking will be reviewed by the Permit Granting Authority.
  24|                    b) All loading docks shall be to the rear of the building(s) and shall not be visibl e from the street. Adequate
  25|                        access for loading and emergency vehicles is maintained on one side of the building.
  26|                3) Sidewalks.
  27|                     a) Sidewalks shall be provided from the street line, when applicable, and from the parking areas to building(s).
  28| 4) Landscaping.
  29|                   a) There shall be a minimum of a four (4) foot landscaped area along the street frontage and along the front and
  30|                         side of the principal building(s) and plantings of indigenous materials along the façades of the building(s) and
  31|                         between the building(s) if there is more than one principal building on site.
  32|                   b) Additional landscaping and screening may be required during the Development Plan Review or by the
  33|                       permitting authority.
  34|                   c) Such landscape shall be a type and height that does not interfere with sight lines of drivers.
  35|                   d) Natural features shall be retained to the extent possible.
  36|                   e) A landscape plan shall be provided and reviewed.
  37|                   f) Where residential neighborhoods abut commercial, office or mixed use developments, appropriate transitional
  38|                       features shall be used and may include landscaping, open space or parks, or streets with clearly designed
  39|                       pedestrian features.
  40| 5) Street Furniture.
  41|                     a) Light fixtures shall be designed to be of number and height that grants plentiful lighting. Such lighting shall
  42|                         shine downwards as to not affect adjacent properties and be dark sky compliant.
  43|                     b)  Lighting must also be placed on the side and rear of the building.
  44|                     c) A Lighting Plan may be required if the Permitting Granting Authority deems the project is of such a scale that
  45|                         the impacts to the neighborhood are adverse; such plan will show where all light fixtures are being proposed
  46|                         within the site, as well as a narrative as to the type of light and height of all fixtures.
  47|                     d) Outdoor tables, benches, and bicycle racks shall be of a style consistent with the principal use(s) of the site.
  48|                     e) The location, number and style of exterior trash receptacles shall be reviewed under Permit Granting
  49|                         Authority.
  50| 4.17.9. Village Center District Campus Developments

===PAGE 166=== (plain, 47 lines)
   1| 166
   2| Developments that contain multiple buildings and uses on a single lot are encouraged as long as they are sensitive to the
   3| context of the surrounding area and meet the purposes outlined above. These developments are subject to the provisions of
   4| Section 8.4 Development Plan Review of the Protective Bylaw as well as the additional design guidelines outlined in Section
   5| 4.17.8. of this Bylaw and below.
   6| a. Additional Design Standards: In addition to those design standards listed in Section 4.17.8. of this bylaw, applications
   7| for a Village Center District Campus shall also meet the following standards:
   8| 1) Buildings
   9| i. Newly constructed building façades for non -residential first floor uses shall have a transparency of at least twenty
  10| (20%) percent and no more than sixty (60%) percent for all facades that, wholly or pa rtially, address street frontage,
  11| sidewalks, or other public space.
  12| ii. Buildings in Village Center District Campus Developments shall meet the following criteria:
  13| a) No more than ten (10%) percent of the first floor of newly constructed buildings shall be occup ied by
  14| residential use.
  15| b) No more than sixty (60%) percent of the total gross floor area of the development shall be occupied by
  16| residential use.
  17| 2) Signs
  18| i. Freestanding directory signs may be permitted as part of a Village Center District Campus application where several
  19| non-residential operations are accessed through a common vehicular entrance. Such freestanding signs shall not
  20| exceed eight (8)
  21| feet in height, six (6) feet in width and each tenant shall be allowed a maximum of four and a half (4.5) square feet to
  22| display the company or agency name.
  23| 3) Site Design
  24| i. Buildings shall be arranged in a manner that optimizes the ability of residents and consumers to access public spaces
  25| and pedestrian amenities.
  26| (ii) Buildings shall be oriented toward each other i n a way that minimizes conflicts between pedestrians and
  27| automobiles.
  28| (iii) Open space provided pursuant to Section 4.17.9.b. of this bylaw shall be designed as a public gathering place.
  29| Arcades, courtyards, parks, greens or other common areas shall be loc ated in a manner that connects buildings to
  30| each other and to public sidewalks without interruption from parking areas or automobile travel lanes to the greatest
  31| practicable extent.
  32| (iv) Features that may be used to create open space areas acceptable to the Planning Board or the Board of Appeals may
  33| include, without limitation, fixed benches, fixed tables, fountains, pathways, bikeways, bicycle racks, period lighting,
  34| shade trees, perennial gardens, picnic areas, and/or trash receptacles.
  35| 4.17.10 Severability
  36| If any provision of this bylaw is held invalid by a court of competent jurisdiction, the remainder of the bylaw shall not be
  37| affected thereby.

  19| Section 1 of Chapter 29C of the General Laws, in the area of Pleasant View Avenue with connection to the sewer
  20| pipe on Graham Street; and to fund this appropriation, to authorize the Town Treasurer with the approval of the
  21| Board of Selectmen to borrow up to $300,000; and further to issue bonds and notes therefor under Chapter 44, and/
  22| or Chapter 29C of the General Laws; and further that the Town Treasurer with the approval of the Board of
  23| Selectmen is authorized to borrow all or a portion of said amount from the Water Pollution Abatement Trust
  24| established pursuant to said Chapter 29C and in connection therewith the Town Treasurer, the Board of Selectmen
  25| and/ or the Sewer Commission is authorized to enter into a financing agreement and/ or security agreement with the
  26| Trust and otherwise to contract with the Trust and the department of Environmental Protection with respect to such
  27| financing and for any federal or state aid available for the project or for the financing thereof and to take any and all
  28| other action including without limitation the acquisition of land or interests therein by purchase or taking by eminent
  29| domain pursuant to General Laws Chapter 79, deemed necessary or advisable for such purposes; that such bonds or
  30| notes shall be general obligations of the Town, provided, however, that it is the intent of the Town that the principal
  31| and interest on such bonds or notes shall be paid, without further appr opriation by the Town to the extent permitted
  32| by law, from fund received by the Town through betterment and/ or privilege fees imposed on abutters who b enefit

  34| ARTICLE 39.  To see if the Town will vote to authorize the enactment of a bylaw regulating the use of vehicles
  35| on Whalom Lake.
  36| Table of Contents
  37| Section 1. Purpose and Scope
  38| Section 2. Definitions
  39| Section 3. Operation of Snow Vehicles and All-Terrain Vehicles
  40| Section 4. Operation of Motor Vehicles prohibited
  41| Section 5. Public Access Only
  42| Section 6. Penalties
  43| Section 1. Purpose and Scope
  44| The Purpose and scope of this bylaw is to protect the safety of the public to prevent the destruction of aquatic life,
  45| waterfowl and other wildlife, the decay of water quality and damage to abutting private and public lands by the
  46| inappropriate use of motor vehicles, recreation vehicles or snow vehicles on the waters or frozen waters of Whalom
  47| Lake; to promote voluntary compliance with all applicable laws relating to the operation of motor vehicles and when
  48| necessary to deter noncompliance through fines and penalties.
  49| Section 2. Definitions
  50| a) Motor Vehicle: any motor vehicle as defined in M.G.L. Chapter 90, § 1.

===PAGE 169=== (plain, 47 lines)
   1| 169
   2| a) All-terrain vehicle: means a motorized recreational vehicle designed or modified for travel on three or
   3| four low pressure tires and having a seat designed to be straddled by the operator and handlebars for
   4| steering control, and recreational vehicle or off-highway vehicle as defined in CMA 323, § 3.02 and
   5| governed by M.G.L.Chapter 90B.
   6| b) Snow vehicle: a motor vehicle designed to travel over ice or snow, having a curb we ight of not more
   7| than 453 kilograms or 21000 pounds, driven by track or tracks in contact with the snow or ice and
   8| steered by a ski or skis in contact with the snow or ice as defined in CMA 323, § 302 and governed by
   9| M.G.L. Chapter 90B.
  10| c) Operation: (1) to ride in or on and control the operation of, or to maintain, suffer, supervise, or permit
  11| the operation of, or to push a snow vehicle or a recreational vehicle, or, (2) In the case of a motor
  12| vehicle, any person who makes use of any mechanical or electrical agency, which alone or in sequence
  13| will set in motion the motive power of the vehicle, whether or not the engine is not running; or (3) any
  14| person who utilizes any combination of actions that would set the vehicle in motion.
  15| d) Public Access: shall mean the public access located in the City of Leominster or any other place where
  16| Whalom Lake can be accessed by the public.
  17| e) Law Enforcement Officer: any police officer, constable or other officer authorized to make arrests or
  18| serve process provided hi is in uniform or displays his/her bade of office.
  19| f) Board: Town of Lunenburg, Board of Selectmen
  20| Section 3. Operation of Snow Vehicles and All-Terrain Vehicles
  21| A. No person, or group of persons shall operate, or cause to be operated on the waters or frozen waters of
  22| Whalom Lake, any snow vehicles that are not in compliance with Massachusetts CMR 323 § 2.01 to
  23| 3.09 & MGL c. 90 § 21 to 35.
  24| B. When the Town of Lunenburg or any other agency or state agency duly authorized by law has posted
  25| the waters or frozen waters of Whalom Lake to prohibit access for or use of snow vehicles or all-
  26| terrain vehicles, no person, or group of persons shall operate any snow vehicle or all -terrain vehicle
  27| upon such waters or frozen waters. In the absence of such a posting, persons utilizing water or t he
  28| frozen water of Whalom Lake do so at their own risk and the Town of Lunenburg assumes no
  29| responsibility either implied or expressed for the safety of any person who voluntarily assumes a
  30| known and obvious risk inherent in such activities. No person shall operate a snow vehicle or all-
  31| terrain vehicle upon any frozen waters of Whalom Lake under any circumstance unless a minimum of
  32| five inches of solid ice cover is present. The operator shall determine the thickness of the ice, and
  33| assumes all responsibility in undertaking an activity with an inherent and known risk.
  34| Section 4. Operation of Motor Vehicles Prohibited
  35| A. No person, group of persons or organizations shall operate, or cause to be operated, any motor vehicle,
  36| as defined in MGL c. 90 § 1 on the waters or frozen waters of Whalom Lake.
  37| Section 5. Public Access Only
  38| B. No Snow Vehicle or All-terrain vehicle shall enter upon Whalom Lake except by public access or by
  39| written permission by the landowner.
  40| Section 6. Penalties
  41| This bylaw may be enforced through any lawful means in law or in equity including, but not limited, to,
  42| enforcement by criminal complaint or fines pursuant to G.L. D.40 § 21D, by the Board of Selectman, the Town
  43| Manager or their duly authorized agents, or any police officer. Whether enforce d through criminal or civil process,
  44| the fine for a violation of this bylaw shall be three hundred dollars ($300.) for each offense. Board of Selectmen to
  45| make recommendation at ATM, Finance Committee reports no direct financial impact.
  46| Motion made and seconded on Article 39 – Point of Order made regarding a quorum being present.  The
  47| meeting recessed for ten minutes and then a count was made – no quorum – meeting dissolved at 3:23PM

  20| ARTICLE 41.    To see if the Town would vote to enact a bylaw authorizing the Board of Selectmen to license
  21| suitable persons to carry on the business of pawnbrokers, buyers and sellers, or dealers in metal, as provided
  22| by M.G.L. c.140  as amended.  .
  23| Table of Contents
  24| Article I. Buyers and Sellers of Goods and Electronics
  25| Section I. Licensing Authority and Requirements
  26| Section II. Interest Rates
  27| Section III. Police may enter and inspect premises and records
  28| Section IV. Numbering of pawned articles
  29| Section V. Maintenance of records; contents; photo identification
  30| Section VI. Pledge cards; contents; retention
  31| Section VII. Copy of required record to pledger
  32| Section VIII. Daily report to police
  33| Section IX. Posting Requirements
  34| Section X. Hours of Operation
  35| Section XI. Disposition of pledged articles
  36| Section XII. Prohibitions
  37| Section XIII. Complaints, Disciplinary Action; Hearing
  38| Section XIV. Suspension or Revocation of License
  39| Article II. Sale, Exchange or Purchase of Metals
  40| Section I. Findings
  41| Section II. Definitions
  42| Section III. License Required
  43| Section IV. Transactions with Minors
  44| Section V. Waiting Period
  45| Section VI. Daily Report
  46| Section VII. Recordkeeping Requirements
  47| Section VIII. Posting of Name, Occupation and License; Inspections
  48| Section IX. Violations and Penalties
  49| Section X. License and Administration Fees
  50| Amendments
  51| Article I. Buyers and Sellers of Goods and Electronics
  52| Section I. Licensing Authority and Requirements
  53| Subsection A.
  54| The Board of Selectmen may license suitable persons to be pawnbrokers or buyer s of goods pursuant to law.
  55| Licenses shall expire on the last day of December annually no matter when obtained during the preceding year. Such
  56| licenses shall not be valid to protect the holders thereof in a building or place other than that designated in the

===PAGE 171=== (plain, 55 lines)
   1| 171
   2| license, unless consent to removal is granted by the Board of Selectmen. Applications f or new licenses under the
   3| rule may be filed at any time with the Board of Selectmen. Applications for the reissue of licenses already existing
   4| should be filed at least 30 days before the expiration of such licenses. Persons whose licenses have expired and have
   5| not been reissued will be liable to prosecution if carrying on business for which the license is required. Every person
   6| so licensed shall, at the time of receiving such license, file a bond as provided by M.G.L. c. 140 § 77 as amended.
   7| Subsection B.
   8| Persons who engage in or carry on the business of lending money on mortgages, deposits or pledges of wearing
   9| apparel, jewelry, ornaments, household goods or other personal property, or of purchasing such property on
  10| condition of selling the same back again at a stipulated price, when the property so mo rtgaged, pledged or purchased
  11| is deposited with the lender, must be licensed as pawnbrokers. This provision, however, does not apply to loans
  12| made on stocks, bonds, notes or other written or printed evidence of ownership of property or of indebtedness to t he
  13| holder or owner of such securities. (M.G.L. c. 140, § 70 – 85)
  14| Section II. Interest Rates
  15| A. Licensed pawnbrokers may charge interest at the rate of 3% per month, and in no event may they charge
  16| more than the rate allowed by the Massachusetts Commissioner of Banks.
  17| B. No such pawnbrokers shall charge or receive any greater rate of interest, and interest shall be determined
  18| on the precise sum advanced by the lender.
  19| C. No pawnbroker shall make or receive any extra charge or fee for storage, care or safekeeping of  any goods,
  20| articles or things pawned with him. (M.G.L. c. 140, § 78)
  21| D.
  22| Section III. Police may enter and inspect premises and records
  23| Any officer of the Lunenburg Police may at any time enter upon any premises used by a licensed pawnbroker or
  24| buyer for the purposes of his or her business, ascertain how he or she conducts his or her business and examine all
  25| articles taken in pawn or bought, or kept or stored in or upon said premises and all books and inventories shall be
  26| exhibited to any above name whenever a demand shall be made for such exhibition. (M.G.L. c. 140, §74) (Penalty
  27| for refusal, M.G.L. c. 140, §74)
  28| Section IV. Numbering of pawned articles
  29| Every licensed pawnbroker or buyer shall at the time of making any loan, attach a number to the article taken in
  30| pawn or bought and shall make entry of such number in the book provided for in Section V. of this article. Section
  31| V. Maintenance of records; contents; photo identification
  32| A. Every licensed pawnbroker or buyer shall keep a book of style and size in which shall be legibly written in
  33| ink in the English language at the time of making each loan, an account and description, including all
  34| distinguishing marks and numbers and serial numbers of the goods, articles or things pledged or pawned,
  35| the amount of money loaned or paid thereon, the day and hour when it was pawned, the rate of i nterest to
  36| be paid on such loan and the number of such article as same. No entry made in said book shall be erased,
  37| obliterated or defaced.  Electronic record keeping by the pawnbroker or buyer is acceptable as long as the
  38| records meet all the bylaw requirements.
  39| B. Every pawnbroker or buyer shall photograph any person pawning or pledging articles and keep such
  40| photographs with said books as part of his or her records. (M.G.L. c. 140, § 79).
  41| C. For all transactions, every pawnbroker or buyer shall also require positive identification and shall record
  42| the type of identification presented and record the date of birth from any person pawning, pledging or
  43| selling any article. Positive identification shall mean any picture identification card issued by a
  44| governmental agency.
  45| D.
  46| Section VI. Pledge cards; contents; retention
  47| Each pledger shall be required by the pawnbroker to sign his/her name and age and address on a card. In the event
  48| that the pledger is unable to write, the pawnbroker shall fill in the name, age and address on such card, together with
  49| a notation stating that the pledger was unable to do so. Such card shall be retained seven years in an alphabetical
  50| index file by the licensed pawnbrokers.
  51| Section VII. Copy of required record to pledger
  52| Every pawnbroker or buyer shall at the time of making such loan deliver to the person who pawns or pledges any
  53| goods, articles or things, a memorandum or note signed by him, containing the subst ance of the entry required to be
  54| made in his book by the provisions of Section V. and no charge shall be made or required by any pawnbroker or
  55| buyer for any such entry, memorandum or note. (M.G.L. c. 140, § 80)

===PAGE 172=== (plain, 57 lines)
   1| 172
   2| Section VIII. Daily report to police
   3|     Subsection A. Every licensed pawnbroker or buyer shall make out and deliver a report of transactions to the
   4| Lunenburg Police Chief or his designee every day before the hour of 10:00 a.m., a legible and correct list containing
   5| an accurate description, including all distinguishable marks, numbers and serial numbers of all articles taken in
   6| pawn or bought during the preceding business day, the respective numbers of such articles as provided by Section
   7| IV of this bylaw, the amount loaned or paid thereon and the time when such articles were pawned or bought. This
   8| report can be done electronically, via the Internet, or faxed to the Lunenburg Police Chief or his designee. If during
   9| the preceding day no articles have been taken in by such pawnbroker or buyer, he or she is required to report
  10| affirmatively. This report can be done both electronically via the Internet and by fax.
  11|    Subsection B. If any goods or articles pawned or pledged and in the possession of a pawnbroker or buyer are
  12| subsequently determined to be stolen property by a member of the Lunenburg Police Department or the
  13| Massachusetts State Police, or if the rightful owner identifies property in the possession of a pawnbroker or buyer
  14| and the property so identified is confirmed to be stolen goods by a member of the Lunenburg Police Department or
  15| Massachusetts State Police, said property shall be returned to the rightful owner at no cost to such owner.
  16| Section IX. Posting Requirements
  17|    Subsection A. Every licensed pawnbroker or buyer shall post in a conspicuous place in his or her shop or office a
  18| copy of the statutes, ordinances and police regulations relating to pawnbrokers/buyers, to be furnished by the
  19| Lunenburg Police Department’s Police Chief or designee, and shall put in some suitable and conspicuous  place in
  20| his shop a sign having his/her name and occupation legibly inscribed thereon in large lettering.
  21|    Subsection B. No pawnbroker or buyer shall place or maintain any signs or devices upon or in connection with his
  22| or her licensed premises indicating or tending to indicate that any form of business is conducted therein which he or
  23| she is not legally authorized to pursue.
  24| Section X. Hours of Operation
  25| Pawnbroker or buyer may have his/her shop open for transactions between sunrise and 10:00 p.m. in the evening of
  26| any weekday and weekend. Under no circumstances can a pawnbroker or buyer operate after the hour of 10:00 p.m.
  27| to sunrise.
  28| Section XI. Disposition of pledged articles
  29|    Subsection A. Articles deposited in pawn with a licensed pawnbroker shall unless redeemed, be retained by
  30| him/her on the premises occupied by him/her for their business for at least four months after the date of deposit.
  31|    Subsection B. After the expiration of the applicable period of time he/she may sell the article by public auction,
  32| apply the proceeds thereof in satisfaction of the debt or demand and the expense of the notice and sale and pay any
  33| surplus to the person entitled thereto on demand.
  34|    Subsection C. No such sale of any article which is not of a perishable nature shall be made unless not less than 10
  35| days prior to the sale a written notice of the intended sale shall have been sent by registered mail to the person
  36| entitled to the payment of any surplus as aforesaid, addressed to his residence as appearing in the records of such
  37| pawnbrokers. Proof of registered mail shall be kept on file for one year after the date of sale.
  38|    Subsection D. No article taken in pawn by such pawnbroker or buyer shall be disposed of otherwise than as above
  39| provided, any agreement or contract between the parties thereto to the contrary notwithstanding.
  40|    Subsection E.  Articles of personal apparel shall not be deemed to be of a perishable nature within the meaning of
  41| this section. (M.G.L. c. 140, § 71)
  42| Section XII. Prohibitions
  43|    Subsection A. No pawnbroker or buyer shall make a loan, directly or indirectly, to a person under the age of 18,
  44| knowing or having reason to believe him or her to be such.
  45|    Subsection B. No pawnbroker or buyer shall loan money secured by deposit or pledge of a firearm, rifle, shotgun
  46| or machine gun. (M.G.L. c. 140, § 131B)
  47|    Subsection C. No pawnbroker or buyer shall hold a license to sell, rent, or lease a firearm, rifle, shotgun, or
  48| machine gun.
  49| Section XIII. Complaints, Disciplinary Action; Hearing
  50|    Subsection A. Complaints concerning licensed pawnbrokers or buyers shall be investigated by the Lunenburg
  51| Police Department under the direction of the Chief of Police. The Chief of Police or his designee will provide a
  52| disciplinary request to the Board of Selectmen for actionable complaints lodged against licensees.
  53|    Subsection B. In the event the Chief of Police determines that disciplinary action should be instituted against a
  54| licensed pawnbroker or buyer, the Chief of Police shall cause to be served upon  the licensed pawnbroker or buyer
  55| notice as to the time, date and place of hearing and of the charge with a statement of the reasons for the proposed
  56| disciplinary action. Hearings shall be conducted in accordance with law.
  57| Section XIV. Suspension or Revocation of License

===PAGE 173=== (plain, 56 lines)
   1| 173
   2| The Board of Selectmen may suspend or revoke licenses provided for by this article for any cause which the Board
   3| of Selectmen deems sufficient. Offenses which may result in the suspension or revocation of a pawnbroker's or
   4| buyer’s license include but are not limited to the following:
   5| a) A violation of any section of this article.
   6| b) A violation of any provision of any statute or applicable law.
   7| Article II. Sale, Exchange or Purchase of Metals
   8| Section I. Findings
   9| The Town of Lunenburg finds that there is a cognizable risk for stolen metals to end up in the possession of even
  10| legitimate businesses which trade in metals, including precious metals. Certain criminals bring stolen metals,
  11| including precious metals to dealers in jewelry and scrap metal. The victims of theft have only small hope of
  12| recovering their property unless the people and entities who receive scrap metals and jewelry are regulated in a
  13| manner which identifies both the metal received by the dealers and the persons who deliver the me tal to these
  14| dealers. There is an Internet alert system used by the Lunenburg Police Department which reports stolen metals.
  15| Monitoring transactions within the town and identifying both the metals and the persons selling or delivering these
  16| metals will serve both to deter theft and to enable the Lunenburg Police Department to trace and recover stolen
  17| property and return it to the rightful owners.
  18| Section II. Definitions
  19| As used in Article II, the following terms shall have the meanings indicated:
  20| ARTICLE: Any metal which is subject to Article II and is exchanged, sold, collected, bartered or received by a
  21| person dealing in metals.
  22| CHIEF OF POLICE:  The Chief of the Lunenburg Police Department or his designee.
  23| EXCHANGE: Receiving, purchasing, selling or bartering metals, including precious metals and industrial scrap
  24| metals.
  25| METAL: A chemical element that is malleable, opaque and ductile, usually solid, which has a characteristic luster,
  26| is a good conductor of heat and electricity, and can be melted or fused, hammered into thin sheets, or drawn into
  27| wires, e.g., copper, aluminum, gold, silver or iron or other metals.
  28| PRECIOUS METAL(S): Any metal which is considered valuable, such as silver, gold, platinum and copper
  29| without regard to the form or amount of such precious metal.
  30| BOARD:  Board of Selectmen
  31| Section III. License Required
  32| a) No person shall collect, deal in, exchange, buy, or keep a shop or place for the exchange, purchase, sale or barter
  33| of metals, including precious metals and industrial metals, in any building or place within the limits of the town
  34| without having a license issued by the Board of Selectman. The license requirement and the other requirements of
  35| this Article II shall not apply to commercial haulers having valid, current United States Department of
  36| Transportation stickers affixed to their vehicles.
  37| b) Board of Selectmen may for a fee set by said board and based upon an application devised by said board, license
  38| suitable persons to be dealers in and keepers of shops or locations for the purchase, sale or barter of precious metals
  39| at such places within the town as may be designated in such license. The Board of Selectmen may place reasonable
  40| conditions on such licenses which shall be inscribed on the face of the license. A license shall be limited to a
  41| specific address stated thereon. Subject to the notice and appeal provisions contained in Section IX (E), (2), (3) of
  42| this Article II, the Board of Selectmen may deny a license to any person who in the exercise of his reasonable
  43| discretion is unsuitable.
  44| c) All metal dealer licenses shall expire annually on the last day in December. The license holders shall be
  45| responsible for submitting an application for license renewal with the license fee to the Board of Selectmen at least
  46| 30 days prior to the last day in December. Upon receipt of an application for renewal, the Board shall consider the
  47| performance of the licensee during the previous year and shall determine whether the licensee had been found in
  48| violation of this Article II and other ordinances and regulations of the town applicable to metal dealers.
  49| d) The Board of Selectmen may promulgate reasonable regulations not inconsistent with this Article II which shall
  50| be binding upon all licensees. No regulation shall take effect until the expiration of 30 days from the date the
  51| regulation(s) is delivered or mailed to the person(s) then licensed. The Board of Selectmen shall deliver a complete
  52| set of regulations and any amendments thereto to the Town Clerk. The Town Clerk shall maintain the complete set
  53| of all regulations in a book available to the public.
  54| e) The obligation to obtain a license as required by this section will take effect 45 days after it is finally enacted.
  55| Section IV. Transactions with Minors
  56| No licensee shall, directly or indirectly, exchange any metals with a person under the age of 18.

===PAGE 174=== (plain, 56 lines)
   1| 174
   2| Section V. Waiting Period
   3| No article exchanged, purchased or received shall be sold, or altered in appearance, form or substance until a peri od
   4| of at least twenty-one (21) days have elapsed from the date of its purchase or receipt. All articles exchanged,
   5| purchased or received must be made available to the Lunenburg Police Department upon request within 24 hours of
   6| such request, and the Police Department may investigate to determine if any article is or may be stolen property.
   7| This waiting period does not apply to scrap metal yards which are exempt from any waiting period.
   8| Section VI. Daily Report
   9| Every licensee shall electronically create and send to the Police Chief or his designee on a form, in the manner and
  10| at the time prescribed by the Police Chief or his designee, a legible complete list containing an accurate description
  11| of all metal articles received by the licensee during the previous 24 hours. The description must include: the type of
  12| metal, the size and/or quantity, whether the article is or may be a precious metal, if applicable; whether the article is
  13| typically used by or associated with a male or female, the wording of any engraving, whether the article has any
  14| gems or precious stones, any identifying number and every other identifiable trait.
  15| Section VII. Recordkeeping Requirements
  16| A. Every licensee shall, at the time of making any exchange or receipt of any article, assign and atta ch a number to
  17| each article received and shall make entry of such number in the book described in Subsection B.
  18| B. Every licensee shall keep a book in which shall be legibly written in ink, in the English language, at the time the
  19| licensee receives any article, the following information:
  20| 1) An account and description, including all distinguishing or identifying marks and numbers of every article
  21| received by the licensee.
  22| 2) The amount of money, if any, paid for the article.
  23| 3) The date and hour when it was received.
  24| 4) The name, age, permanent address and telephone number of the person from who purchased or received.
  25| 5) A copy of the document used to identify the person giving the article to the licensee.
  26| C. Every licensee shall require positive identification from any person selling or exchanging any article. The
  27| licensee shall copy and record the type of identification presented and the date of birth from any person giving or
  28| exchanging any article. Positive identification means only a government-issued document with the person's name
  29| and photograph. The licensee shall photograph any person exchanging any article(s). A copy of the identification
  30| and the photograph shall be kept with the book required by this section as part of his records.
  31| D. No entry made in the book required by this section shall be erased, obliterated or defaced in any manner
  32| whatsoever. The book shall at all times be open for the inspection by the Police Chief or any police officer. The
  33| licensee shall keep and maintain all the records required by this Article II for a period of three years from the date of
  34| the last entry in any book.
  35| Section VIII. Posting of Name, Occupation and License; Inspections
  36| Every licensee shall post in some suitable and conspicuous place where he or she receives or exchanges articles, a
  37| sign having his/ her full legal name, and his/her business name, if different, and his/her occupation legibly described
  38| thereon in large letters. The current license shall also be so posted. The location where transactions occur, the store
  39| or shop and all articles exchanged and or kept therein or received therein and the book and documents required by
  40| this Article II, may at all times be examined by the Police Chief or any police officer  at their request.
  41| Section IX. Violations and Penalties
  42| A. Whoever not having a valid license as required by this Article II, exchanges, collects, deals in or barters metals in
  43| any place or manner other than that designated in his license, or after notice to him that his license has been revoked,
  44| or whoever violates this Article II or any rule, regulation or restriction contained in his license, or any regulation
  45| promulgated by the Town of Lunenburg shall be subject to the fines and sanctions described in t his section. Each
  46| day of such violation shall constitute a separate offense.
  47| B. The fine for breach of the duty imposed by this Article II is three hundred dollars ($300). Each day on which the
  48| person continues in violation shall be considered as a separate violation.
  49| C. This Article II may at the discretion of the Chief of Police be enforced by civil process or by noncriminal
  50| disposition as provided in M.G.L. c. 40, § 21D. The Chief of Police or any Lunenburg police officer may issue
  51| tickets to enforce this Article II. If enforced civilly by ticket each day on which a violation exists shall be d eemed to
  52| be a separate offense and the violator shall be subject to the following fines:
  53|        1) First violation: $150.
  54|        2) Second violation: $200.
  55|        3) Third violation: $300.
  56|        4) Fourth and each subsequent violation: $300.

===PAGE 175=== (plain, 55 lines)
   1| 175
   2| D.  In addition to the penalties set forth above, the enforcing authority may seek an injunction from an appropriate
   3| court to restrain any violation of this Article II, any regulation promulgated by the Town of Lunenburg or any
   4| condition inscribed on a license.
   5| E. The Board of Selectmen may terminate any license or refuse to renew any license for violation of this Article II, a
   6| regulation promulgated by the Town of Lunenburg or any condition inscribed on the license. The Board of
   7| Selectmen shall use the procedure described in this section.
   8|      1) Notice of violation. Whenever it comes to the attention of the Board of Selectmen that a violation of this
   9| Article II, a regulation promulgated by the Town of Lunenburg or a condition inscribed on a license, the Board shall
  10| by written notice, deliver personally or send by certified mail to the licensee notifying him of the violation and order
  11| the person to cease and desist. Depending on the severity of the violation as determined by him, the Board of
  12| Selectmen may terminate the license. The Board shall also provide notice described herein if it denies an application
  13| for a license stating the reasons therefor.
  14|       2) Contents of notice. The written notice to the licensee shall contain a description of the violation(s) and the
  15| date(s) of occurrence and a citation to the section of the ordinance, regulation or license condition which the licensee
  16| is alleged to have violated. The notice will, if applicable, describe what the licensee must do to comply, whether the
  17| Board of Selectmen intends to terminate the license and the effective date of the termination which shall afford th e
  18| licensee at least seven days to file an appeal of the proposed termination with the Board. In the case of denial of a
  19| license, the notice shall state the reasons for the denial.
  20|      3) Appeal. Upon receipt of a notice of license termination, or denial, the licensee may, within seven days of
  21| receipt of the notice, file a written appeal by delivery or certified mail to the Board of Selectmen's office during the
  22| hours of 8:30 a.m. to 4:30 p.m. on days when Town Hall is open for business. When the Board of Selectme n
  23| receives an appeal, they shall appoint a neutral hearing officer who will take testimony from any persons with
  24| knowledge of the facts including any police officer and the appellant. The appellant may be represented by counsel
  25| and any person so testifying will be subject to cross examination. The proceedings wil l be governed by the rules of
  26| evidence applicable to an informal administrative hearing. The hearing officer will assure that an appropriate record,
  27| including any documents submitted, is made and kept. The decision of the hearing officer will be written an d final.
  28| F. The Board of Selectmen, subject to the process described in this section, may refuse to renew any license. The
  29| Board shall not issue a license to any person convicted of a felony.
  30| Section X. License and Administration Fees
  31|             1) Second Hand Buyer of Goods and Electronics Annually $250.00
  32|             2) Second Buyer of Metal and Precious Metals Annually $250.00
  33|             3) Pawnbroker License Annually $100.00
  34| Amendments
  35| (Submitted by Police Chief) Board of Selectmen recommends approval. Finance Committee reports no direct
  36| financial impact.
  37| ARTICLE 42.   To see if the Town will vote to enact a bylaw to restrict Sexual Offender Residency and create
  38| Sex Offender Safety Zones
  39| Table of Contents
  40| Section 1 Findings and Intent
  41| Section 2 Definitions
  42| Section 3 Sex Offender Residence Prohibition
  43| Section 4 Evidentiary Matters; Measurements
  44| Section 5 Notice to Move
  45| Section 6 Exceptions
  46| Section 7 Forfeiture of Exception
  47| Section 8 Penalties
  48| Section 9 Safety Zone
  49| Section 10 Maps and List of Safety Zones
  50| Section 11 Severability
  51| Section 1. Findings and Intent
  52| A. It is the intent of this bylaw to serve and to protect the Town's compelling interest to promote, protect
  53| and improve the health, safety and welfare of the citizens of the Town by creating areas around
  54| locations where children, or the elderly regularly congregate in concentrated numbers wherein certain
  55| sex offenders are prohibited from loitering and establishing temporary or permanent residence.

===PAGE 176=== (plain, 59 lines)
   1| 176
   2| A. After careful consideration, the Town finds that this bylaw is the most narrowly tailored means of
   3| limiting, to the fullest extent possible, the opportunity for certain sex offenders to approach or otherwise
   4| come in contact with children or the elderly in places where children or the elderly would natural ly
   5| congregate, and that the protection of the health and safety of our children and elderly is a compelling
   6| governmental interest.
   7| B. By the enactment of this or any other bylaw, the Town understands that it cannot remove the t hreat
   8| posed to or guarantee the safety of children or the elderly or assure the public that sex offenders will
   9| comply with the mandates of this bylaw. This legislation is intended to create a civil, non -punitive
  10| regulatory scheme in order to protect children and the elderly to the extent possible under the
  11| circumstances and not as a punitive measure of any kind.
  12| C. Certain sex offenders pose a clear threat to the children and the elderly residing or visiting in the
  13| community. Because certain sex offenders are more likely than any other type of offender to reoffend by
  14| another sexual offense, the Town desires to impose safety precautions in furtherance of the goal of
  15| protecting the children and elderly. The purpose of this bylaw is to reduce the potential risk of harm to
  16| children and the elderly in the community by limiting the ability of certain classified levels of sex
  17| offenders to be in contact with unsuspecting children and the elderly in locations that are primarily
  18| designed for use by or are primarily used by children and the elderly, the grounds of a public or private
  19| school for children, a center of facility that provides day care or children's services, a park, other public
  20| recreational facility, elderly housing facilities or the Senior Citizen’s Center. The Town desires to add
  21| location restrictions to such offenders to the extent state law is silent.
  22| Section 2.  Definitions
  23| 1) “Camp Bus Stop” Any area designated by a private/public youth camp as a camp bus stop whic h has been
  24| designated in a list maintained by the Town and available to the public.
  25| 2) “Day Care Center” means an establishment, whether public or private, which provides care for children and
  26| is registered with and licensed pursuant to the laws of the Commonwealth of Massachusetts by the
  27| Department of Early Education and Care.
  28| 3) “Elder” or “Elderly” means a person or persons over 60 years of age. “Elderly Housing Facility” or “Senior
  29| Citizens Center” or “Over 55 Community” means any building or buildings which provide a group
  30| residence for the elderly or a location where the elderly gather and/or reside that is located within the Town
  31| of Lunenburg, Massachusetts.
  32| 4) “Establishing a Residence” means to set up or bring into being a dwelling place or an abode where a person
  33| sleeps, which may include more than one location, and may be mobile or transitory, or by means of
  34| purchasing real property or entering into a lease or rental agreement for real property.
  35| 5)  “Loitering” means to remain for more than fifteen (15) minutes within a five hundred (500) foot distance
  36| of the location in question.
  37| 6) “Park” means active and passive public land designated for recreational or athletic use by the Town of
  38| Lunenburg, Massachusetts and located within the Town of Lunenburg Massachusetts.
  39| 7) “Permanent Residence” means a place where a person lives, abides, lodges, or resides for five (5) or more
  40| consecutive days or fourteen (14) or more days in the aggregate during any calendar year.
  41| 8) “Recreational Facility” means a playground, a forest preserve, conservation area, jogging trail or running
  42| track, hiking or biking trail, beach, water park, swimming pool, wading pool, soccer field, baseball field,
  43| football field, basketball court or hockey rink, whether publicly or privately owned, to which the public has
  44| a right of access as an   invitee and which is located within the Town of Lunenburg, Massachusetts.
  45| 9) “School” means any public or private educational facility that provides services to children in grades
  46| Kindergarten - 12, or any one or more of such grades.
  47| 10) “School Bus Stop” means any area designated by the public school district or by a private or parochial
  48| school within the Town of Lunenburg, Massachusetts as a school bus stop, which school bus stop has been
  49| designated in a list maintained by the Town and available to the public.
  50| 11) “Sex Offender” and “Sex offense” shall have the same meaning for purposes of this bylaw as provided for
  51| in G.L. c. 6, § 178C.
  52| 12) “Sex Offense Involving a Child” shall have the same meaning for purposes of this bylaw as provided for in
  53| G.L. c. 6, § 178C.
  54| 13) “Temporary Residence” means a place where a person lives, abides, lodges or resides for a period of less
  55| than five (5) consecutive days or fourteen (14) days in the aggregate during any calendar year.
  56| Section 3. Sex Offender Residence Prohibition
  57| It is unlawful for any sex offender who has been finally classified as a Level 2 or 3 offender pursuant to the
  58| guidelines of the Sex Offender Registry Board, for as long as so classified, to establish a permanent residence or
  59| temporary residence in the Town of Lunenburg, Massachusetts within one  tho usand (1,000) feet of the property on

===PAGE 177=== (plain, 60 lines)
   1| 177
   2| which any public or private school, park, playground, beach, biking/hiking trail, other recreational facility, elderly
   3| housing facility, over 55 community, senior citizens center,  or licensed day-care center is located.
   4| Section 4.  Evidentiary Matters; Measurements
   5| For purposes of determining the minimum distance requirement, the separation shall be measured by following a
   6| straight line from the outer property line of the permanent residence to the nearest outer propert y line of any public
   7| or private school, park, playground, beach, biking/hiking trail, other recreational facility, elderly housing facility,
   8| over 55 community, senior citizens center, or licensed day-care center.
   9| Section 5.  Notice to Move
  10| Any sex offender who has  been finally classified as a Level 2 or 3 offender pursuant to the guidelines of the Sex
  11| Offender Registry Board, for as  long as so classified, who establishes a permanent residence or temporary residence
  12| in the Town of Lunenburg, Massachusetts within one thousand (1,000) feet of any public or private school, park,
  13| playground, beach, biking/hiking trail, other recreational facility, elderly housing facility, over 55 community, senior
  14| citizens center,  or licensed day-care center shall be in violation of this bylaw and shall, within thirty (30) days of
  15| receipt of written notice of the sex offender’s noncompliance with this bylaw, move from said location to a new
  16| location, but said location may not be within one thousand (1,000) feet of any public o r private school, park,
  17| playground, beach, biking/hiking trail, other recreational facility, elderly housing facility, over 55 community, senior
  18| citizens center,  or licensed day-care center within the Town of Lunenburg, Massachusetts.  Furthermore it shal l be a
  19| separate violation each day that a sex offender shall move from one location in the Town of Lunenburg to another
  20| that is within one thousand (1,000) feet of any public or private school, park, playground, beach, biking/hiking trail,
  21| other recreational facility, elderly housing facility, over 55 community, senior citizens center, or licensed day -care
  22| center.
  23| Section 6.  Exceptions
  24| A sex offender residing within 1,000 feet of any public or private school, park, playground, beach, biking/hiking
  25| trail, other recreational facility, elderly housing facility, over 55 communities, senior citizens center, or licensed day -
  26| care center does not commit a violation of this bylaw if any of the following apply:
  27| 1) The person established the permanent residence and reported and registered the residence, as required by
  28| the Sex Offender Registry Law and any applicable regulations of the Massachusetts Sex Offender
  29| Registry Board, prior to the effective date of this  bylaw, and:
  30| a) Permanent residence was established by purchasing the real property where the residence is established,
  31| as long as the registered sex offender continues to reside in and does not move to another restricted
  32| location in Lunenburg different from the permanent residence established prior to the effective  date of
  33| this bylaw;
  34| b) Permanent residence was established through a valid, fixed-term, written and/or oral lease or rental
  35| agreement, executed prior to the effective date of this bylaw, as long as the registered sex offender
  36| continues to reside within and does not move to another restricted location in Lunenburg different from
  37| the permanent residence established prior to the effective date of this bylaw; or
  38| c) Permanent residence was established through a written and/or oral lease or rental agreement at the will
  39| of the landlord, as long as the registered sex offender continues to reside within and does not move to
  40| another restricted location in Lunenburg different from the permanent residence established prior to the
  41| effective date of this bylaw.
  42|             2)   The person was a minor when the relevant crime was committed and was not convicted as an adult.
  43|             3)   The person is a minor.
  44| 4)  The public or private school, park, playground, beach, biking/hiking trail, other recreational facility,
  45| elderly housing facility, over 55 community, senior citizens center, or licensed day -care center was
  46| established after such person established the permanent residence and reported and registered the residence
  47| pursuant to the Sex Offender Registry Law and any applicable regulations of the Massachusetts Sex
  48| Offender Registry Board.
  49| 5) The person is required to serve a sentence at a jail, prison, juvenile facility, or other correctional
  50| institution or facility located within the aforementioned 1,000 foot area.
  51| 6) The person is admitted to and/or subject to an order of commitment at a public or private facility for the
  52| care and treatment of mentally ill persons pursuant to G.L. c. 123 located within the aforementioned 1,000
  53| foot area.
  54| 7) The person is a mentally ill person subject to guardianship pursuant to order or supervision of the
  55| Probate and Family Court or a mentally retarded person subject to guardianship pursuant to G.L. c. 201
  56| §6A, residing with his or her guardian or residing within a group residence that is professionally staffed and
  57| supervised 24 hours a day and located within the aforementioned 1,000 foot area.
  58| Section 7. Forfeiture of Exception
  59| If, either after the effective date of this bylaw or after a new public or private school, park, playground, beach,
  60| biking/hiking trail, other recreational facility, elderly housing facility, over 55 community, senior

===PAGE 178=== (plain, 57 lines)
   1| 178
   2| citizens center, or licensed day-care center opens, an indictment or conviction of another sex offense is
   3| issued by a court against a Level 2 or 3 Sex Offender otherwise enjoying an exception under this bylaw, he
   4| or she shall immediately forfeit that exception and be required to comply with this bylaw.
   5| Section 8. Penalties
   6| 1) Criminal Complaint.  Violation of the residency provisions of this bylaw may be enforced by criminal
   7| complaint filed by any police officer of the Town of Lunenburg, Massachusetts.  Each day a violation
   8| exists shall constitute a separate violation.  The fine for each violation upon conviction shall be three
   9| hundred dollars ($300).
  10| 2) Noncriminal Disposition.  In addition to enforcement by criminal complaint, violation of the residency
  11| provisions of this bylaw may be enforced through any lawful means in law or in equity by any police
  12| officer of the Town of Lunenburg including, but not limited to, enforcement by non-criminal
  13| disposition pursuant to G.L. c. 40, § 21D.  Each day a violation exists shall constitute a separate
  14| violation. The penalties for noncriminal disposition shall be as follows:
  15| a) First offense: Notification to offender that he/she has thirty (30) days to move.
  16| b) Subsequent offense: noncriminal fine of three hundred dollars ($300), enforceable by a
  17| police officer, and written notification to the property owner, if other than the offender, the
  18| offender’s landlord, parole officer and/or probation officer, and the Commonwealth's Sex
  19| Offender Registry Board that the sex offender has violated a town bylaw.
  20| Section 9. Safety Zone
  21| A. Prohibitions
  22| A sex offender who has been finally classified as a Level 2 or 3 offender pursuant to the guidelines of the Sex
  23| Offender Registry Board, and for as  long as so classified, is prohibited from entering upon the premises of a school
  24| or day-care center unless previously authorized specifically in writing by the school  administration or day-care
  25| center owner.
  26| A sex offender who has been finally classified as a Level 2 or 3 offender pursuant to the guidelines of the Sex
  27| Offender Registry Board, and for as long as so classified, is prohibited from entering upon the premis es of an elderly
  28| housing facility, over 55 Community or Senior Citizens Center, unless previously authorized in writing by the on -
  29| site manager of the elderly housing facility, over 55 Community or Senior Citizen Center.
  30| A sex offender who has been finally classified as a Level 2 or 3 offender pursuant to the guidelines of the Sex
  31| Offender Registry Board, and for as  long as so classified, is prohibited from entering upon the premises of a park or
  32| any recreational facility. A sex offender who has been finally classified as a Level 2 or 3 offender pursuant to the
  33| guidelines of the Sex Offender Registry Board, and for as  long as so classified, after having received notice from
  34| the Lunenburg Police Department that he/she is loitering by having remained for more than fifteen (15) minutes
  35| within five hundred (500) feet of a public or private school, park,  playground, beach, biking/hiking trail, other
  36| recreational facility, elderly housing facility, over 55 community, senior citizens center, or licensed day -care center,
  37| is prohibited from continuing to so loiter. For purposes of determining the minimum distance separation under this
  38| section, the distance shall be measured by following a straight line from the registered sex offender to the outer
  39| property line of the public or private school, park, playground, beach, biking/hiking trail, other recreational facility,
  40| elderly housing facility, over 55 community, senior citizens center, or licensed day -care center.
  41| A sex offender who has been finally classified as a Level 2 or 3 offender pursuant to the guidelines of the Sex
  42| Offender Registry Board, and for as  long as so classified after having received notice from the Lunenburg Police
  43| Department that he/she is loitering by having remained for more than fifteen (15) minutes within five hundred (500)
  44| feet of a school and/or camp bus stop which has been designated in a list maintained by the Town and available to
  45| the public is prohibited from continuing to so loiter; provided, however, that this prohibition shall no t apply on days
  46| when the schools and/or camps within the Town of Lunenburg are not in session.
  47| A. Exceptions
  48| 1) The prohibitions defined in this bylaw shall not be construed or enforced so as to prohibit a sex offender
  49| from exercising his or her right to vote in any federal, state or municipal election, conducting town and/or
  50| police business or from attending any religious service.
  51| 2) The prohibitions defined in this bylaw do not apply to a sex offender's place of residence when such
  52| residence is accepted under this bylaw.
  53| B. Penalties
  54| 1) Criminal Complaint.  Violation of the safety zone provisions of this bylaw may be enforced by
  55| criminal complaint filed by any police officer of the Town of Lunenburg.  Each day a violation exists
  56| shall constitute a separate violation.  The fine for each violation, upon conviction, shall be three
  57| hundred dollars ($300).

===PAGE 179=== (plain, 43 lines)
   1| 179
   2| 2) Noncriminal Disposition.  In addition to enforcement by criminal complaint, violation of the safety zone
   3| provisions of this bylaw may be enforced through any lawful means in law or in equity by any police
   4| officer of the Town of Lunenburg including, but not limited to, enforcement by non -criminal disposition
   5| pursuant to G.L. c. 40, § 21D.  Each day a violation exists shall constitute a separate violation.  The
   6| penalties for noncriminal disposition shall be as follows:
   7| a) First offense: noncriminal fine of one hundred and fifty dollars ($150), enforceable by a Lunenburg police officer.
   8| b) Subsequent offense: noncriminal fine of three hundred dollars ($300), enforceable  by a Lunenburg police officer,
   9| and written notification to the offender’s parole officer and/or probation officer, and the Commonwealth's Sex
  10| Offender Registry Board that the sex offender has violated a Town Bylaw.
  11| Section 10. Maps and List of Safety Zones
  12| One or more maps depicting the prohibited residency restriction areas defined by this bylaw and depicting the safety
  13| zone areas defined by this bylaw shall be created by the Town and maintained by the Lunenburg Police Department.
  14| A written list describing the prohibited areas defined by this bylaw, including school and/or camp bus stops, shall be
  15| created by the Town and maintained by the Lunenburg Police Department.  As to school and/or camps bus stops, the
  16| list shall govern over the maps. The list, maps and a copy of this bylaw shall be available to the public at the
  17| Lunenburg Police Department, the Lunenburg Town Clerk’s office and on the Town of Lunenburg’s website.  The
  18| Town shall review the list and maps annually for changes.
  19| Section 11. Severability
  20| If any portion of this bylaw is deemed by a court of competent jurisdiction to be unconstitutional or otherwise
  21| invalid or unenforceable, such judgment shall not impair or invalidate or render unenforceable the remaining
  22| portions of this bylaw. (Submitted by Police Chief) Board of Selectmen to make recommendation at ATM, Finance
  23| Committee reports no direct financial impact.
