  41| ARTICLE 8.  To see if the Town will vote to authorize the enactment of a bylaw regulating the use of vehicles on
  42| Whalom Lake.
  43| Table of Contents
  44| Section 1. Purpose and Scope
  45| Section 2. Definitions
  46| Section 3. Operation of Snow Vehicles and All-Terrain Vehicles
  47| Section 4. Operation of Motor Vehicles prohibited
  48| Section 5. Access
  49| Section 6. Penalties

===PAGE 181=== (plain, 59 lines)
   1| 181
   2| Section 1. Purpose and Scope
   3| The purpose and scope of this bylaw is to protect the safety of the public, to prevent the dest ruction of  aquatic life, waterfowl
   4| or other wildlife, the decay of water quality and damage to abutting  private and public lands by the inappropriate use of
   5| motor vehicles, recreation vehicles or snow vehicles on the waters or frozen waters of Whalom Lak e; to promote voluntary
   6| compliance with all applicable laws relating to the operation of motor vehicles and when necessary to deter noncompliance
   7| through fines and penalties.
   8| Section 2.  Definitions
   9| a) Motor Vehicle: any motor vehicle as defined in M.G.L Chapter 90, § 1.
  10| b) All-terrain vehicle: means a motorized recreational vehicle designed or modified for travel on four low pressure
  11| tires and having a seat designed to be straddled by the operator and handlebars for steering control, and recreational
  12| vehicle or off-highway vehicle as defined in CMR 323, § 3.02 and governed by M.G.L Chapter 90B.
  13| c) Snow vehicle: a motor vehicle designed to travel over ice or snow, having a curb weight of not more than 453
  14| kilograms or 1000 pounds, driven by track or tracks in contact with the snow or ice and steered by a ski or skis in
  15| contact with the snow or ice as defined in CMR 323, § 302 and governed by M.G.L Chapter 90B.
  16| d) Operation: to ride in or on and control the operation of, or to maintain, suffer, supervise, or permit the o peration of,
  17| or to push a snow vehicle or an all- terrain vehicle, or in the case of a motor vehicle, any person who makes use of
  18| any mechanical or electrical agency, which alone or in sequence will set in motion the motive power  of the vehicle,
  19| or if the engine is not running, any person who utilizes any combination of actions that would set the vehicle in
  20| motion.
  21| e) Public Access: shall mean the public access located in the City of Leominster or any other place where Whalom
  22| Lake can be accessed by the public.
  23| f) Law Enforcement Officer: any police officer, constable or other officer authorized to make arrests or serve process
  24| provided he is in uniform or displays his/her badge of office.
  25| g) Board: Town of Lunenburg, Board of Selectmen
  26| Section 3. Operation of Snow Vehicles and All-Terrain Vehicles
  27| A. No person, or group of persons shall operate, or cause to be operated on the waters or frozen waters of Whalom
  28| Lake, any snow vehicles that are not in compliance with Massachusetts CMR 323 § 2.01 to 3.09 & M .G.L. c. 90
  29| § 21 to 35.
  30| B. When the Town of Lunenburg or any other agency or state agency duly authorized by law has posted the waters or
  31| frozen waters of Whalom Lake to prohibit access for or use of snow vehicles or all-terrain vehicles, no person, or
  32| group of persons shall operate any snow vehicle or all-terrain vehicle upon such waters or frozen waters. In the
  33| absence of such a posting, persons utilizing water or the frozen water of Whalom Lake do so at their own risk and
  34| the Town of Lunenburg assumes no responsibility either implied or expressed for the safety of any person who
  35| voluntarily assumes a known and obvious risk inherent in such activities. No person shall operate a snow vehicle or
  36| all-terrain vehicle upon any frozen waters of Whalom Lake under any circumstan ce unless a minimum of five inches
  37| of solid ice cover is present. The operator shall determine the thickness of the ice and assumes all responsibility in
  38| undertaking an activity with an inherent and known risk.
  39| Section 4. Operation of Motor Vehicles Prohibited
  40| A. No person, group of persons or organizations shall operate, or cause to be operated, any motor vehicle as defined in
  41| M.G.L. c. 90 § 1 on the waters or frozen waters of Whalom Lake.
  42| Section 5. Access
  43| B. No snow vehicle or all-terrain vehicle shall enter upon Whalom Lake except by public access or by written
  44| permission by the landowner.
  45| Section 6. Penalties
  46| This bylaw may be enforced through any lawful means in law or in equity including, but not limited to, enforcement  by
  47| criminal complaint pursuant to G.L. c.40 § 21D, by the Board of Selectmen, the Town Manager, or their duly authorized
  48| agents, or any police officer. The fine for a violation of this bylaw shall be three hundred dollars ($300.) for each offense.
  49| (Submitted by Police Chief) Board of Selectmen recommends approval. Finance Committee reports no direct financial
  50| impact.  VOTED with amendment to Section 3.B adding the words “In the absence of such a posting,” to the beginning
  51| of the second sentence.

  44| Amendments
  45| Article I. Buyers and Sellers of Goods and Electronics
  46| Section I. Licensing Authority and Requirements
  47| Subsection A.
  48| The Board of Selectmen may license suitable persons to be pawnbrokers or buyer s of goods pursuant to law. Licenses shall
  49| expire on the last day of December annually no matter when obtained during the preceding year. Such licenses shall not be
  50| valid to protect the holders thereof in a building or place other than that designated in the license, unless consent to remo val
  51| is granted by the Board of Selectmen. Applications for new licenses under the rule may be filed at any time with the Board of
  52| Selectmen. Applications for the reissue of licenses already existing should be filed at least 30 days before the expiration o f
  53| such licenses. Persons whose licenses have expired and have not been reissued will be liable to prosecution if carrying on
  54| business for which the license is required. Every person so licensed shall, at the time of receiving such license, file a bon d as
  55| provided by M.G.L. c. 140 § 77 as amended.

===PAGE 183=== (plain, 45 lines)
   1| 183
   2| Subsection B.
   3| Persons who engage in or carry on the business of lending money on mortgages, deposits or pledges of wearing apparel,
   4| jewelry, ornaments, household goods or other personal property, or of purchasing such property on condition of selling the
   5| same back again at a stipulated price, when the property so mortgaged, pledged or purchased is deposited with the lender,
   6| must be licensed as pawnbrokers. This provision, however, does not apply to loans made on stocks, bonds, notes or other
   7| written or printed evidence of ownership of property or of indebtedness to the holder or owner of such securities. (M .G.L. c.
   8| 140, § 70 – 85)
   9| Section II. Interest Rates
  10| A. Licensed pawnbrokers may charge interest at the rate of 3% per month, and in no event may they charge more than
  11| the rate allowed by the Massachusetts Commissioner of Banks.
  12| B. No such pawnbrokers shall charge or receive any greater rate of interest, and interest shall be determined on the
  13| precise sum advanced by the lender.
  14| C. No pawnbroker shall make or receive any extra charge or fee for storage, care or safekeeping of any goods, articles
  15| or things pawned with him. (M.G.L. c. 140, § 78)
  16| Section III. Police may enter and inspect premises and records
  17| Any officer of the Lunenburg Police may at any time enter upon any premises used by a licensed pawnbroker or buyer for the
  18| purposes of his or her business, ascertain how he or she conducts his or her business and examine all articles taken in pawn or
  19| bought, or kept or stored in or upon said premises and all books and inventories shall be exhibited to any above name
  20| whenever a demand shall be made for such exhibition. (M.G.L. c. 140, §74) (Penalty for refusal, M.G.L. c. 140, §74)
  21| Section IV. Numbering of pawned articles
  22| Every licensed pawnbroker or buyer shall at the time of making any loan, attach a number to the article taken in pawn or
  23| bought and shall make entry of such number in the book provided for in Section V. of this article.
  24| Section V. Maintenance of records; contents; photo identification
  25| A. Every licensed pawnbroker or buyer shall keep a book of style and size in which shall be legibly written in ink in
  26| the English language at the time of making each loan, an account and description, including all distinguishing
  27| marks and numbers and serial numbers of the goods, articles or things pledged or pawned, the amount of money
  28| loaned or paid thereon, the day and hour when it was pawned, the rate of i nterest to be paid on such loan and the
  29| number of such article as same. No entry made in said book shall be erased, oblit erated or defaced.  Electronic
  30| record keeping by the pawnbroker or buyer is acceptable as long as the records meet all the bylaw requirements.
  31| B. Every pawnbroker or buyer shall photograph any person pawning or pledging articles and keep such photographs
  32| with said books as part of his or her records. (M.G.L. c. 140, § 79).
  33| C. For all transactions, every pawnbroker or buyer shall also require positive identification and shall record the type of
  34| identification presented and record the date of birth from any person pawning, pledgin g or selling any article.
  35| Positive identification shall mean any picture identification card issued by a governmental agency.
  36| Section VI. Pledge cards; contents; retention
  37| Each pledger shall be required by the pawnbroker to sign his/her name and age and address on a card. In the event that the
  38| pledger is unable to write, the pawnbroker shall fill in the name, age and address on such card, together with a notation
  39| stating that the pledger was unable to do so. Such card shall be retained seven years in an al phabetical index file by the
  40| licensed pawnbrokers.
  41| Section VII. Copy of required record to pledger
  42| Every pawnbroker or buyer shall at the time of making such loan deliver to the person who pawns or pledges any goods,
  43| articles or things, a memorandum or note signed by him, containing the substance of the entry required to be made in  his
  44| book by the provisions of Section V. and no charge shall be made or required by any pawnbroker or buyer for any such entry,
  45| memorandum or note. (M.G.L. c. 140, § 80)

===PAGE 184=== (plain, 54 lines)
   1| 184
   2| Section VIII. Daily report to police
   3|     Subsection A. Every licensed pawnbroker or buyer shall make out and deliver a report of transactions to the Lunenbur g
   4| Police Chief or his designee every day before the hour of 10:00 a.m., a legible and correct list containing an accurate
   5| description, including all distinguishable marks, numbers and serial numbers of all articles taken in pawn or bought during
   6| the preceding business day, the respective numbers of such articles as provided by Section IV . of this bylaw, the amount
   7| loaned or paid thereon and the time when such articles were pawned or bought. This report can be done electronically , via the
   8| Internet, or faxed to the Lunenburg Police Chief or his designee. If during the preceding day no articles have been taken i n by
   9| such pawnbroker or buyer, he or she is required to report affirmatively. This report can be done both electronically via the
  10| Internet and by fax.
  11|    Subsection B. If any goods or articles pawned or pledged and in the possession of a pawnbroker or buye r are subsequently
  12| determined to be stolen property by a member of the Lunenburg Police Department or the Massachusetts State Police, or if
  13| the rightful owner identifies property in the possession of a pawnbroker or buyer and the property so identified is confirmed
  14| to be stolen goods by a member of the Lunenburg Police Department or Massachusetts State Police, said property shall be
  15| returned to the rightful owner at no cost to such owner.
  16| Section IX. Posting Requirements
  17|    Subsection A. Every licensed pawnbroker or buyer shall post in a conspicuous place in his or her shop or office a copy of
  18| the statutes, ordinances and police regulations relating to pawnbrokers/buyers, to be furnished by the Lunenburg Police
  19| Department’s Police Chief or designee, and shall put in some suitable and conspicuous place in his shop a sign having his/her
  20| name and occupation legibly inscribed thereon in large lettering.
  21|    Subsection B. No pawnbroker or buyer shall place or maintain any signs or devices upon or in connection wit h his or her
  22| licensed premises indicating or tending to indicate that any form of business is conducted therein which he or she is not
  23| legally authorized to pursue.
  24| Section X. Hours of Operation
  25| Pawnbroker or buyer may have his/her shop open for transactions between sunrise and 10:00 p.m. in the evening of any
  26| weekday and weekend. Under no circumstances can a pawnbroker or buyer operate after the hour of 10:00 p.m. to sunrise.
  27| Section XI. Disposition of pledged articles
  28|    Subsection A. Articles deposited in pawn with a licensed pawnbroker shall unless redeemed, be retained by him /her on the
  29| premises occupied by him/her for their business for at least four months after the date of deposit.
  30|    Subsection B. After the expiration of the applicable period of time he/she may sell the article by public auction, apply the
  31| proceeds thereof in satisfaction of the debt or demand and the expense of the notice and sale and pay any surplus to the
  32| person entitled thereto on demand.
  33|    Subsection C. No such sale of any article which is not of a perishable nature shall be made unless not less than 10 days
  34| prior to the sale a written notice of the intended sale shall have been sent by registered mail to the person entitled to the
  35| payment of any surplus as aforesaid, addressed to his residence as appearing in the records of such pawnbrokers. Proof of
  36| registered mail shall be kept on file for one year after the date of sale.
  37|    Subsection D. No article taken in pawn by such pawnbroker or buyer shall be disposed of otherwise than  as above
  38| provided, any agreement or contract between the parties thereto to the contrary notwithstanding.
  39|    Subsection E.  Articles of personal apparel shall not be deemed to be of a perishable nature within the meaning of this
  40| section. (M.G.L. c. 140, § 71)
  41| Section XII. Prohibitions
  42|    Subsection A. No pawnbroker or buyer shall make a loan, directly or indirectly, to a person under the age of 18, knowing
  43| or having reason to believe him or her to be such.
  44|    Subsection B. No pawnbroker or buyer shall loan money secured by deposit or pledge of a firearm, rifle, shotgun or
  45| machine gun. (M.G.L. c. 140, § 131B)
  46|    Subsection C. No pawnbroker or buyer shall hold a license to sell, rent, or lease a firearm, rifle, shotgun, or machine gun.
  47| Section XIII. Complaints, Disciplinary Action; Hearing
  48|    Subsection A. Complaints concerning licensed pawnbrokers or buyers shall be investigated by the Lunenburg Police
  49| Department under the direction of the Chief of Police. The Chief of Police or his designee will provide a disciplinary request
  50| to the Board of Selectmen for actionable complaints lodged against licensees.
  51|    Subsection B. In the event the Chief of Police determines that disciplinary action should be instituted against a licensed
  52| pawnbroker or buyer, the Chief of Police shall cause to be served upon the licensed pawnbroker or buyer notice as to the
  53| time, date and place of hearing and of the charge with a statement of the reasons for the proposed disciplinary action.
  54| Hearings shall be conducted in accordance with law.

===PAGE 185=== (plain, 54 lines)
   1| 185
   2| Section XIV. Suspension or Revocation of License
   3| The Board of Selectmen may suspend or revoke licenses provided for by this article for any cause which the Board of
   4| Selectmen deems sufficient. Offenses which may result in the suspension or revocati on of a pawnbroker's or buyer’s license
   5| include but are not limited to the following:
   6| a) A violation of any section of this article.
   7| b) A violation of any provision of any statute or applicable law.
   8| Article II. Sale, Exchange or Purchase of Metals
   9| Section I. Findings
  10| The Town of Lunenburg finds that there is a cognizable risk for stolen metals to end up in the possession of even legitimate
  11| businesses which trade in metals, including precious metals. Certain criminals bring stolen met als, including precious metals
  12| to dealers in jewelry and scrap metal. The victims of theft have only small hope of recovering their property unless the people
  13| and entities who receive scrap metals and jewelry are regulated in a manner which identifies both the metal received by the
  14| dealers and the persons who deliver the metal to these dealers. There is an Internet alert system used by t he Lunenburg Police
  15| Department which reports stolen metals. Monitoring transactions within the town and identifying both the metals and the
  16| persons selling or delivering these metals will serve both to deter theft and to enable the Lunenburg Police Department to
  17| trace and recover stolen property and return it to the rightful owners.
  18| Section II. Definitions
  19| As used in Article II, the following terms shall have the meanings indicated:
  20| ARTICLE: Any metal which is subject to Article II and is exchanged, sold, collected, bartered or received by a person
  21| dealing in metals.
  22| CHIEF OF POLICE:  The Chief of the Lunenburg Police Department or his designee.
  23| EXCHANGE: Receiving, purchasing, selling or bartering metals, including precious metals and industrial scrap metals.
  24| METAL: A chemical element that is malleable, opaque and ductile, usually solid, which has a characteristic luster, is a good
  25| conductor of heat and electricity, and can be melted or fused, hammered into thin sheets, or drawn into wires, e.g., copper,
  26| aluminum, gold, silver or iron or other metals.
  27| PRECIOUS METAL(S): Any metal which is considered valuable, such as silver, gold, platinum and copper without regard
  28| to the form or amount of such precious metal.
  29| BOARD:  Board of Selectmen
  30| Section III. License Required
  31| a) No person shall collect, deal in, exchange, buy, or keep a shop or place for th e exchange, purchase, sale or barter of metals,
  32| including precious metals and industrial metals, in any building or place within the limits of the town without having a
  33| license issued by the Board of Selectman. The license requirement and the other require ments of this Article II shall not
  34| apply to commercial haulers having valid, current United States Department of Transportation stickers affixed to their
  35| vehicles.
  36| b) Board of Selectmen may for a fee set by said board and based upon an application devised by said board, license suitable
  37| persons to be dealers in and keepers of shops or locations for the purchase, sale or barter of precious metals at such places
  38| within the town as may be designated in such license. The Board of Selectmen may place reasonable conditions on such
  39| licenses which shall be inscribed on the face of the license. A license shall be limited to a specific address stated thereon .
  40| Subject to the notice and appeal provisions contained in Section IX (E),(2),(3) of this Article II, the Board of Selectmen may
  41| deny a license to any person who in the exercise of his reasonable discretion is unsuitable.
  42| c) All metal dealer licenses shall expire annually on the last day in December. The license holders shall be responsible for
  43| submitting an application for license renewal with the license fee to the Board of Selectmen at least 30 days prior to the last
  44| day in December. Upon receipt of an application for renewal, the Board shall consider the performance of the licensee during
  45| the previous year and shall determine whether the licensee had been found in violation of this Article II  and other ordinances
  46| and regulations of the town applicable to metal dealers.
  47| d) The Board of Selectmen may promulgate reasonable regulations not inconsistent with this Artic le II which shall be binding
  48| upon all licensees. No regulation shall take effect until the expiration of 30 days from the date the regulation(s) is delive red or
  49| mailed to the person(s) then licensed. The Board of Selectmen shall deliver a complete set of r egulations and any
  50| amendments thereto to the Town Clerk. The Town Clerk shall maintain the complete set of all regulations in a book available
  51| to the public.
  52| e) The obligation to obtain a license as required by this section will take effect 45 days after it is finally enacted.
  53| Section IV. Transactions with Minors
  54| No licensee shall, directly or indirectly, exchange any metals with a person under the age of 18.

===PAGE 186=== (plain, 52 lines)
   1| 186
   2| Section V. Waiting Period
   3| No article exchanged, purchased or received shall be sold, or altered i n appearance, form or substance until a period of at
   4| least twenty-one (21) days have elapsed from the date of its purchase or receipt. All articles exchanged, purchased or received
   5| must be made available to the Lunenburg Police Department upon request with in 24 hours of such request, and the Police
   6| Department may investigate to determine if any article is or may be stolen property. This waiting period does not apply to
   7| scrap metal yards which are exempt from any waiting period.
   8| Section VI. Daily Report
   9| Every licensee shall electronically create and send to the Police Chief or his designee on a form, in the manner and at the time
  10| prescribed by the Police Chief or his designee, a legible complete list containing an accurate description of all metal articles
  11| received by the licensee during the previous 24 hours. The description must include: the type of metal, the size and/or
  12| quantity, whether the article is or may be a precious metal, if applicable; whether the article is typically used by or associated
  13| with a male or female, the wording of any engraving, whether the article has any gems or precious stones, any identifying
  14| number and every other identifiable trait.
  15| Section VII. Recordkeeping Requirements
  16| A. Every licensee shall, at the time of making any exchange or receipt of any article, assign and attach a number
  17|      to each article received and shall make entry of such number in the book described in Subsection B.
  18| B. Every licensee shall keep a book in which shall be legibly written in ink, in the English lang uage, at the time
  19|      the licensee receives any article, the following information:
  20|   1) An account and description, including all distinguishing or identifying marks and numbers of every article
  21|      received by the licensee.
  22|   2) The amount of money, if any, paid for the article.
  23|   3) The date and hour when it was received.
  24|   4) The name, age, permanent address and telephone number of the person from who purchased or received.
  25|   5) A copy of the document used to identify the person giving the article to the licensee.
  26|   C. Every licensee shall require positive identification from any person selling or exchanging any article. The
  27|       licensee shall copy and record the type of identification presented and the date of birth from any person giving
  28|       or exchanging any article. Positive identification means only a government-issued document with the person's
  29|       name and photograph. The licensee shall photograph any person exchanging any article(s). A copy of the
  30|       identification and the photograph shall be kept with the book required by this section as part of his records.
  31|   D. No entry made in the book required by this section shall be erased, obliterated or defaced in any manner
  32|       whatsoever. The book shall at all times be open for the inspection by the Police Chief or any police officer.
  33|       The licensee shall keep and maintain all the records required by this Article II for a period of three years from
  34|       the date of the last entry in any book.
  35|   Section VIII. Posting of Name, Occupation and License; Inspections
  36| Every licensee shall post in some suitable and conspicuous place where he or she receives or exchanges articles, a sign
  37| having his/ her full legal name, and his/her business name, if different, and his/her occupation legibly described thereon in
  38| large letters. The current license shall also be so posted. The location where transactions occur, the store or shop and all
  39| articles exchanged and or kept therein or received therein and the book and documents required by this Article II, may at all
  40| times be examined by the Police Chief or any police officer at their request.
  41| Section IX. Violations and Penalties
  42| A. Whoever not having a valid license as required by this Article II, exchanges, collects, deals in or barters metals in any
  43| place or manner other than that designated in his license, or after notice to him that his license has been revoked, or whoever
  44| violates this Article II or any rule, regulation or restriction contained in his license, or any regulation promulgated by th e
  45| Town of Lunenburg shall be subject to the fines and sanctions described in this section. Each day of such violation shall
  46| constitute a separate offense.
  47| B. The fine for breach of the duty imposed by this Article II is three hundred dollars ($300). Each day on which the person
  48| continues in violation shall be considered as a separate violation.
  49| C. This Article II may at the discretion of the Chief of Police be enforced by civil process or by noncriminal disposition as
  50| provided in M.G.L. c. 40, § 21D. The Chief of Police or any Lunenburg police officer may issue tickets to enforce this
  51| Article II. If enforced civilly by ticket each day on which a violation exists shall be d eemed to be a separate offense and the
  52| violator shall be subject to the following fines:

===PAGE 187=== (plain, 52 lines)
   1| 187
   2| 1) First violation: $150.
   3|        2) Second violation: $200.
   4|        3) Third violation: $300.
   5|        4) Fourth and each subsequent violation: $300.
   6| D.  In addition to the penalties set forth above, the enforcing authority may seek an injunction from an appropriate court to
   7| restrain any violation of this Article II, any regulation promulgated by the Town of Lunenburg or any condition inscribed on
   8| a license.
   9| E. The Board of Selectmen may terminate any license or refuse to renew any license for violation of this Article II, a
  10| regulation promulgated by the Town of Lunenburg or any condition inscribed on the license. The Boa rd of Selectmen shall
  11| use the procedure described in this section.
  12|      1) Notice of violation. Whenever it comes to the attention of the Board of Selectmen that a  violation of this Article II, a
  13| regulation promulgated by the Town of Lunenburg or a condition inscribed on a license, th e Board shall by written notice,
  14| deliver personally or send by certified mail to the licensee notifying him of the violation and order the person to cease and
  15| desist. Depending on the severity of the violation as determined by him, the Bo ard of Selectmen may terminate the license.
  16| The Board shall also provide notice described herein if it denies an application for a license stating the r easons therefor.
  17|       2) Contents of notice. The written notice to the licensee shall contain a description of the v iolation(s) and the date(s) of
  18| occurrence and a citation to the section of the ordinance, regulation or license condition which the licen see is alleged to have
  19| violated. The notice will, if applicable, describe what the licensee must do to comply, whether the Board of Selectmen
  20| intends to terminate the license and the effective date of the termination which shall afford th e licensee at least seven days to
  21| file an appeal of the proposed termination with the Board. In the case of denial of a license, the notice shall state the reasons
  22| for the denial.
  23|      3) Appeal. Upon receipt of a notice of license termination, or denial, the licensee may, within seven days of receipt of the
  24| notice, file a written appeal by delivery or certified mail to the Board of Selectmen's office during the hours of 8:30 a.m. to
  25| 4:30 p.m. on days when Town Hall is open for business. When the Board of Selectme n receives an appeal, they shall appoint
  26| a neutral hearing officer who will take testimony from any persons with knowledge of the facts including any police officer
  27| and the appellant. The appellant may be represented by counsel and any person so testifying will be s ubject to cross
  28| examination. The proceedings will be governed by the rules of evidence applicable to an informal administrative hearing. The
  29| hearing officer will assure that an appropriate record, including any documents submitted, is made and kept. The de cision of
  30| the hearing officer will be written and final.
  31| F. The Board of Selectmen, subject to the process described in this section, may refuse to renew any license. The Board shall
  32| not issue a license to any person convicted of a felony.

  35| PREAMBLE
  36| This by-law establishes a Salary Administration Plan, hereinafter called the “Plan”, which classifies several positions of town
  37| services into groups, establishes salary schedules, establishes so called “fringe” benefits, or “indirect pay” provisions, such as
  38| holidays and vacations with pay, sick leave, and the like, and establishes a Personnel Committee to administer the Plan, as
  39| hereinafter provided, and to represent the interest of both taxpayers and town employees.
  40| SECTION 1.  THE PERSONNEL COMMITTEE
  41| There is hereby established a Personnel Committee consisting of five members, each residing in the Town of Lunenburg, to
  42| be appointed by the Selectmen and Town Manager, and each member shall serve for a t erm of three years; provided,
  43| however, that of the members originally appointed, one shall serve a term of one year, two for a term of two years, and two
  44| for a term of three years, as designated by the Selectmen and Town Manager.  However, nothing herein s hall be deemed to
  45| prohibit the chairman of the Personnel Committee from designating a member of said committee to serve ex officio as
  46| representative of said Committee on a study committee of the Town Meeting, nor shall such service affect the tenure of sai d
  47| member of the Personnel Committee.  If any member of the Personnel Committee shall resign or otherwise vacate the office
  48| before the expiration of their term, the successor shall be appointed by the Selectmen and Town Manager, at their discretion,
  49| to serve for the balance of the unexpired term.
  50| A Town employee may serve as an ex officio member of the Personnel Committee and may be called upon from time to time
  51| to provide information requested by the Personnel Committee so that the Personnel Committee can  make well informed
  52| decisions and proposals.
  53| Said Committee shall elect its own chairman and vice-chairman by September 15th of each year; and in the absence or
  54| incapacity of the chairman, the vice-chairman shall act as chairman.  The Committee shall keep a record of its official
  55| proceedings and actions, may establish its own rule of procedure and may, subject to appropriation therefore, retain such

===PAGE 190=== (plain, 47 lines)
   1| 190
   2| clerical and other assistance, and make such other expenditures as may be necessary to the performance of its duties.  Three
   3| members shall constitute a quorum for the transaction of the business of the committee, and the affirmative vote of three
   4| members shall be necessary to any official act of the Committee.
   5| The Personnel Committee is hereby authorized to a dminister the provisions of this by -law, except for such duties as may be
   6| specifically assigned by statute or by -law to other Town officers, boards or committees.  Appeals for decisions of the
   7| Personnel Committee may be taken to the Town at the next Annual or Special Town Meeting.
   8| The Personnel Committee may from time to time issue, amend, or revoke Administrative Orders for the purpose of giving
   9| effect to the provisions of this by -law, and for the purpose of giving effect to the provisions of such other b y-laws and votes
  10| of the Town, pertaining to personnel administration, as said Committee may be responsible for administering and/or
  11| enforcing.  Each such order shall be numbered in sequence, and said Committee shall maintain in the Town Office a file for
  12| all such orders issued, said file to be open to public inspection.
  13| Except as otherwise provided by law, the Committee shall have access to all facts, figures, records and other information
  14| relating to the personnel of Town departments other than the Schoo l Department, and the same shall be furnished forthwith
  15| by any such department whenever so requested by the Personnel Committee, in such a form as said Committee may require.
  16| In the event that the Personnel Committee does not have enough members to conduc t business, the Town Manager shall have
  17| the authority to administer the Salary Administration Plan.
  18| SECTION 2.  CLASSIFICATION AND SALARY SCHEDULE
  19| The positions of all officers except those part -time administrative positions required by statute, and emplo yees in the service
  20| of the Town, whether probationary, full -time, part -time, hourly, seasonal, intermittent, and any others, other than those
  21| positions filled by popular election, all contractual employees, all police officers under the rank of Police Chie f, and those
  22| under the direction and control of the School Committee, are hereby classified by titles in the groups listed in Schedule A,
  23| entitled “Classification and Salary Schedule”, as set forth in this by-law.
  24| SECTION 3.  TITLE OF POSITIONS
  25| No person shall be appointed, employed or paid as an employee in any position subject to the provision of the Salary
  26| Administration Plan under any title other than those of the Classification and Salary Schedule, or under any title other than
  27| that of the job the dut ies of which are actually performed.  The job title in the Classification and Salary Schedule of the Plan
  28| shall be the official title for all purposes having to do with the position and shall be used to designate the position in al l
  29| payrolls, budget estimates and official reports, and in every other connection involving personnel and fiscal processes.
  30| SECTION 4.  NEW OR CHANGED POSITIONS
  31| The Personnel Committee tentatively may add new job title and descriptions to this Plan, establishing grades with minimu m
  32| and maximum wage or salary brackets for the same, and reclassifying existing jobs, revising the description and the
  33| minimum and maximum wage and salary brackets therefore, and such addition or reclassification to be effective only for the
  34| period from the  date of such action by the Personnel Committee to the date of any vote with respect thereto adopted at a
  35| subsequent Town Meeting but in no case later than the next Annual Town Meeting unless such later date as may be specified
  36| by such vote.  Any increase in wages or salaries under this section shall be effective only if funds are available for the
  37| purpose, and approved by the Finance Committee and Board of Selectmen.
  38| SECTION 5.  RECLASSIFICATION OF EMPLOYEES
  39| No employee shall be reclassified to a job in a nother group, either higher or lower, until the Personnel Committee shall have
  40| determined that such a reclassification will be consistent with the provisions of the Salary Administration Plan.
  41| SECTION 6.  JOB DESCRIPTIONS AND INTERPRETATIONS
  42| The Personnel Committee shall maintain written job descriptions of the jobs or positions in the Plan, describing the essential
  43| functions, requirements and duties.  The descriptions shall not be interpreted as either complete or restrictive and employee s
  44| shall continue to perform any duties assigned by an employee’s supervisor.
  45| Every three (3) years, the Personnel committee shall review the wage and salary schedules of all positions in the Plan, and b e
  46| in a position to recommend to the Board of Selectmen any action deemed desirable to maintain a fair and equitable pay level.
  47| SECTION 7.  SALARY SCHEDULE

===PAGE 191=== (plain, 48 lines)
   1| 191
   2| The Classification and Salary Schedule of the Salary Administration Plan, set forth in Schedule A of this by -law, shall consist
   3| of the maximum and minimum salaries and s tep-rate salaries for the groups and positions in the Classification and Salary
   4| Schedule.  The salary range of a group shall be the salary range for all positions classified in such group.
   5| Irrespective of the period of time for which pay rates may be show n in the Salary Schedule, all employees shall continue to
   6| be paid on an hourly, weekly, monthly, or other basis, as at present, unless otherwise authorized by the Personnel Committee
   7| or by amendment of the Salary Administration Plan.
   8| SECTION 8.  SALARY RATES ABOVE MAXIMUM
   9| Any salary rate which is above the maximum rate for a job, as established by Salary Administration Plan, shall be deemed to
  10| be a personal rate, and apply only to the incumbent.  When such incumbent leaves the employ of the Town or is tran sferred to
  11| another job with a higher maximum rate than their personal rate or higher maximum rates are established, the personal rate
  12| shall disappear.  No other employee assigned to or hired for such job shall advance beyond the maximum of the job.
  13| SECTION 9.  LAYOFF PROCEDURE – RECALL PROCEDURE
  14| Layoff Procedure  – If the Town decides to lay  off employees, it shall determine from which classification(s) or job
  15| function(s) layoffs shall be made.  Layoffs shall then be made based on seniority within the class ification.
  16| “Seniority” shall be the length of an employee’s continuous service measured from the employee’s most recent appointment
  17| date; provided an employee’s seniority may be reduced for periods of unpaid leave, unless otherwise prohibited by law.
  18| If two or more employees in the same classification have the same seniority, the employee who has the shorter term of
  19| continuous employment with the Town shall be laid off.  If there are two or more employees in the same classification with
  20| the same seniority and the same term of continuous employment with the Town, a part -time employee shall be laid off before
  21| a full-time employee.  If there are still two or more employees in the same classification, the Town Manager shall determine
  22| which employee shall be laid off.
  23| An employee that is scheduled to be laid off shall have the right to bump other employees in lower classifications with less
  24| seniority, provided that the Town Manager, in his/her sole discretion, but with consultation of the Department Head, sh all
  25| make a determination that the employee retained is qualified and able to perform the duties of the lower classification, subj ect
  26| to disability discrimination laws.
  27| Employees who have been laid off from employment are responsible for keeping the Town a dvised of their current address
  28| for the purpose of potential recall from layoff.
  29| Recall Policy – If the town conducts a recall from layoff, recall shall be offered in reverse order of layoff by classification
  30| provided the laid off employee is qualified an d able to perform the duties of the classification to which the employee is being
  31| offered recall, subject to disability discrimination laws.
  32| Employees shall only be eligible for recall from layoff under the terms of this policy for one (1) year from the da te of the
  33| employee’s layoff.
  34| Any employee who is recalled from layoff under the terms of this policy shall retain his/her seniority, less the period of
  35| layoff.
  36| The Town will notify an employee of a recall opportunity by certified letter (or other verifia ble method of delivery) to the
  37| employee’s last known address.  An employee who has been offered an opportunity to be recalled from layoff must advise
  38| the Town of his/her intention to accept recall within five (5) working days.
  39| SECTION 10.  TRANSFERS AND PROMOTIONS
  40| (a)When an employee is promoted to a job with a higher rate range, or rate of pay, he/she shall enter it at the minimum of the
  41| job rate range or at their own rate, whichever is the higher, but in no event at a rate in excess of the maximum for s uch job.
  42| He/she may also receive at the time one or more step rate increases, provided the maximum for the job is not exceeded, if the
  43| Department Head recommends that qualification and performance warrant it, and the Personnel Committee approves.  If the
  44| Department Head should feel that there should be a trial period before  recommending the promotional step rate increase or
  45| increases, on recommendation of the Department Head, the Personnel Committee may approve any such deferred
  46| promotional increase at the conclusion of the trial period.
  47| (b)If an employee is transferred to a job with a lower rate range, or rate of pay, he/she shall enter it at their own rate or at  the
  48| maximum rate for the job, whichever is the lower.

===PAGE 192=== (plain, 54 lines)
   1| 192
   2| SECTION 11.  NEW PERSONNEL
   3| (a) The hiring rate shall be the minimum of the rate range of the job for which the new employee if hired, unless otherwise
   4| authorized by the Personnel Committee.  Department Heads shall notify the Personnel Committee of the hiring of all new
   5| personnel.  This notification shall be done in writing via the New Hire Personnel Form.
   6| (b)  Effective July 1, 2015, for new regular full -time, and new regular part -time personnel for whose position there is a rate
   7| range, eligibility for a step increase will depend on the employe e’s date of hire.  If hired between January 1 st and June 30 th,
   8| the employee is not eligible for a step increase until July 1 st of the following calendar year.  If hired between July 1 st and
   9| December 31st, the employee is eligible for their first step increase on the upcoming July 1st.
  10| SECTION 12.  GRIEVANCE PROCEDURE
  11| Any employee who believes that they have received inequitable treatment is encouraged to discuss the grievance initially
  12| with their supervisor within 10 working days of the occurrence or kno wledge of the event.  If the employee is not
  13| comfortable discussing the matter with their direct supervisor, or if the matter is not settled, the employee should submit t he
  14| said grievance to their Department Head.  The Department Head shall meet with the e mployee and will respond in writing to
  15| the employee within 14 days of the meeting.
  16| If the grievance is not resolved by the decision of the Department Head, the employee may file an appeal in writing with the
  17| Town Manager within 10 days of receipt of the d ecision.  The Town Manager will meet with the employee within a
  18| reasonable time after receipt of the appeal and will respond in writing to the employee’s grievance within 30 days after
  19| meeting with the employee.
  20| SECTION 13.  DEPARTMENT BUDGETS
  21| Each Department Head shall include in the annual budget request, a pay adjustment section to provide funds for anticipated
  22| pay adjustments during the ensuing year, expenditures to be made therefrom only in accordance with the Salary
  23| Administration Plan with approval of the Personnel Committee.
  24| SECTION 14.  SEVERABILITY PROVISION
  25| In the event any provision of this by -law or application thereof shall be held to be invalid by the proper authorities, this shall
  26| not be construed to affect the validity of any other provision, or application thereof, of this by-law.
  27| SECTION 15.  CIVIL SERVICE
  28| Nothing in this by -law of Salary Administration Plan shall be construed to conflict with Chapter 31 (Civil Service) of the
  29| General Laws.
  30| SECTION 16.  HOURS OF WORK
  31| The schedule of hours to be worked shall be determined by the appropriate Department Head.
  32| For positions entitled to overtime pay at one and one -half (1 1/2) times the regular rate for work actually performed in excess
  33| of forty (40) hours within a work week, the words “wor k actually performed” shall be construed to include all duly paid time
  34| off, including but not limited to holidays, sick leave, vacation, personal days and bereavement leave, and such leave shall b e
  35| included in the computation of forty (40) hours.
  36| Unauthorized leave or leave for which the employee is not entitled to compensation shall not be included in such
  37| computation.
  38| I. Industrial Trades and Service Occupations
  39|  A. Work Week 40 hours in 5 days.
  40|  B. One and one-half (1 1/2) times the regular rate will be paid for work actually performed in
  41|    excess of forty (40) hours within a work week.
  42| II. Office Occupations
  43|  A. Work Week - Forty (40) hours in five days and may be reduced administratively to 36 hours in
  44|    four or five days.
  45|  B. One and one-half (1 1/2) times the regular rate will be paid for work actually performed in
  46|    excess of forty (40) hours within a work week.
  47| III. Public Safety Occupations
  48| A. Fire Department - The hours of the Fire Department shall be determined by the Chief with the approval of
  49| the Personnel Committee provided, however, that all hours worked by any member
  50| of the Fire Department will be paid for at the regular rate then in force.
  51| IV. Administrative and Professional Occupations
  52|  A. Persons employed in these positions are not governed by hours of the positions under their
  53|   supervision or control.  Generally, such positions shall be for at least forty (40) hours per week
  54|   when full-time and not entitled to overtime.

===PAGE 193=== (plain, 51 lines)
   1| 193
   2| Notwithstanding anything in the foregoing paragraphs, the appr opriate department head may, instead of paying the overtime
   3| rate, provide that the employee take compensatory time off at a rate of 1 1/2 times the hours worked that qualify for overtim e
   4| pay.  Compensatory time must be taken within 60 days.
   5| SECTION 17.  PAID VACATIONS
   6| All persons employed regularly by the Town full -time or part -time for a minimum of twenty (20) hours per week shall
   7| receive paid vacations according to the following schedule. Any persons working between 20 and 40 hours per week shall
   8| receive paid vacations, but it will be prorated accordingly.  The part -time compensation formula shall be used to determine
   9| the prorated part-time employee’s vacation day (See Section 28).
  10| Earned vacation time up to and including 80 hours must be used in the y ear granted.  No unused portion of this allowance
  11| shall be carried forward into a subsequent vacation year, nor shall any monetary compensation be given for such unused
  12| portion.  Earned vacation time in excess of 80 hours should be used in the year granted , and no portion of such excess shall
  13| be carried forward into a subsequent year.  If departmental scheduling or other serious extenuating circumstances preclude
  14| the use of all such excess in the year granted, the employee shall receive compensation equival ent to such unused portion in a
  15| lump sum payable on the last payroll warrant in June, in addition to their regular salary; provided, however, that such
  16| payment must be approved by the Department Head and written authorization signed by the Personnel Commit tee in advance
  17| of such payment.
  18| Employment Termination.  An employee whose employment ends, whether voluntary resignation, retirement or involuntary
  19| termination, shall receive compensation equivalent to earned vacation allowance, provided he/she has been in the continuous
  20| service of the Town for 10 months, as follows:
  21| (a) All currently available, unused vacation allowance up to a maximum of  200 hours; and (b) One twelfth (1/12) of the
  22| vacation allowance which would otherwise have been credited on the next suc ceeding July 1, for each full month
  23| worked since the preceding July 1.
  24| The vacation year shall begin July 1.  The assignment of vacation leave shall be arranged by the Department Head for such
  25| time or times as best serve the public interest, provided, ho wever, that each employee shall be entitled to use at least 40 hours
  26| of their vacation accrual during the period between June 1 and September 15 if desired, based on seniority.
  27| No vacation time may be used in advance of the July 1 date on which it would n ormally be credited; except that where such
  28| advance served the needs both of the employee and of the Town, it may be requested by the Department Head and authorized
  29| in writing by the Town Manager.  In no case shall any such advanced vacation exceed the num ber of days for which the
  30| employee would be entitled to compensation, were he/she to terminate their services in good standing on the first day of such
  31| vacation, in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 5, subparagraph (b), of this section.
  32| SECTION 18.  PAID HOLIDAYS
  33| The Town of Lunenburg shall observe the following holidays:
  34| New Year’s Day Columbus Day
  35| Martin Luther King Day Veterans’ Day
  36| After Completion of: Hours of Paid Vacation
  37| (Based on 40 hour week)
  38| 10 Months 80
  39| 1 Year 80
  40| 5 Years 120
  41| 10 Years 160
  42| 11 Years 164
  43| 12 Years 168
  44| 13 Years 172
  45| 14 Years 176
  46| 15 Years 180
  47| 16 Years 184
  48| 17 Years 188
  49| 18 Years 192
  50| 19 Years 196
  51| 20+ Years 200

===PAGE 194=== (plain, 49 lines)
   1| 194
   2| Washington’s Birthday Thanksgiving Day
   3| Patriots Day The Day after Thanksgiving
   4| Memorial Day The Day before Christmas
   5| Independence Day Christmas Day
   6| Labor Day
   7| It is the intent of this section that no employee, employed full -time or part -time for a minimum  of twenty (20) hours, shall
   8| lose pay by the observance of any of the listed holidays; provided, how ever, that part -time employees shall receive holiday
   9| benefits only for holidays falling within their regularly scheduled work week; and provided further that, except as specifica lly
  10| provided elsewhere in this section, no employee, full -time or part-time, shall receive, in any week in which a holiday occurs,
  11| compensation in excess of his or her normal week’s pay as a result of the payment of compensation for such holiday.  To
  12| these ends, this section shall be administered as follows:
  13| Full-Time Employees (36 -40 hours):  The employee is entitled to each of the scheduled holidays or the equivalent time off
  14| with pay, based on the hourly rate of the employee for the number of hours normally worked on the day of the week on
  15| which the holiday falls, or for the numb er of hours normally worked on the day of the week given as equivalent time off, as
  16| the case may be.
  17| Part-Time Employees (minimum 20 hours):  The employee is entitled to compensation for those holidays falling within his or
  18| her usual work schedule only, as follows:
  19| a. The number of hours normally scheduled for the day of week on which the holiday falls; or
  20| b. The number of hours normally scheduled for each week, divided by the number of days  normally
  21| scheduled to work in each week, whichever is applicable.
  22| Holiday time will be given on the holiday if possi ble.  If not, where applicable, equivalent time off with pay will be granted.
  23| However, if according to the needs of the Department, holiday time or equivalent time off cannot be granted, the employee
  24| shall receive payment at one and one -half times the regular hourly rate for all hours worked on the holiday, in addition to his
  25| or her holiday pay.  Whenever a holiday falls on Sunday, it will be observed on the Monday following.  Whenever a holiday
  26| falls on Saturday or on a full -time employee’s day off, holiday time will be scheduled by the Department Head or the
  27| employee will receive a Floating Holiday.  The Floating Holiday is equal to eight (8) hours for employees who regularly
  28| work 40 hours per week, and will be prorated according to the Part -time compensation formula for those who work less than
  29| 40 hours.    Holiday time will not be cumulative and must be taken within either three (3) weeks before or three (3) weeks
  30| following the specified holiday.
  31| SECTION 19.  SICK LEAVE
  32| All persons employed, full-time or part-time who have completed the six (6) months probationary period by July 1 of any
  33| year, shall be entitled to sick leave of ninety-six (96) hours,  to be credited on July 1.  The part-time compensation formula
  34| shall be used to determine the employee’s prorated sick pay (See Section 28).
  35| New employees having completed the six (6) months probationary period shall be credited as of the end of said six (6)
  36| months, with sick leave in the amount of eight (8) hours for each full month intervening between the six months anniversary
  37| date and the next July 1, up to the maximum of ninety-six (96) hours.  Use of such leave must be occasioned by sickness or
  38| injury of the employee or their immediate family member.
  39| Unused sick leave shall be cumulative up to a maximum of 1,200 hours, and shall be carried forward on July 1 of each year,
  40| to be added to the sick leave credited as of July 1. Sick leave may be donated to another employee who has exhausted their
  41| accumulated sick leave at the discretion of the Town Manager.
  42| When an employee finds it necessary to be absent because of accident or illness, he/she shall report the fact to their
  43| immediate supervisor as soon as possible either in person or by agent.  Sick lea ve will not be granted unless such report is
  44| made.  For the protection of the Town, the Department Head or supervisor may require the presentation of a doctor’s
  45| certificate in connection with a claim for sick leave that exceeds, or is equal to 24 hours (or  three missed days of work), and
  46| may, if it is deemed advisable, send a doctor or nurse to investigate any absence alleged to be caused by illness.  Any
  47| employee who refuses to submit to a doctor’s examination shall not be entitled to sick leave.
  48| An employee who retires in good standing shall receive compensation based on a percentage of his/her accumulated unused
  49| sick leave, up to a maximum of 480 hours, as follows:  5 years continuous service 10%; 15 years continuous service 50%; 25

===PAGE 195=== (plain, 50 lines)
   1| 195
   2| years continuous service 75%.  In the event of an employee’s death, the above benefit will be paid to his/her beneficiary. This
   3| retirement benefit will not be available to any employee hired after June 30, 2010.
   4| Sick Time can be used for any of the following reasons:
   5|  Care for the employee’s own physical or mental illness, injury or medical condition that requires home care or rest,
   6| professional medical diagnosis or care, or preventative medical care;
   7|  Care for the employee’s child, spouse, parent or parent of a spouse, who is suffering from a physical or mental
   8| illness, injury, or medical condition that requires home care, professional medical diagnosis or care, or preventive
   9| medical care;
  10|  Attend the employee’s routine medical appointment or a routine medical appointment for the  employee’s child,
  11| spouse, parent, or parent of spouse.
  12| SECTION 20.  PERSONAL LEAVE
  13| All persons employed, full time or part time who have completed six (6) months probationary period by July 1 of any year,
  14| shall be entitled to personal leave of twenty -four (24) hours, to become available on July 1 for use during the ensuing fiscal
  15| year.  The part -time compensation formula shall be used to determine the employee’s prorated personal hours (See Section
  16| 26).
  17| New employees having completed the six (6) months p robationary period shall be credited as of the end of said six (6)
  18| months, with eight (8) personal hours for use during the period between said six (6) months anniversary and the end of the
  19| then current fiscal year; provided, however, that the said six (6)  months probationary period must have been completed by
  20| May 1 of that year.
  21| Employees who do not use any sick leave within a three month (3) month period (calculated quarterly), will be entitled to
  22| eight (8) additional personal hours for each such quarter ; said personal hours are to be used in the same fiscal year in which
  23| they are earned.  Personal leave shall be scheduled with the approval of the Department Head.  Personal leave shall not be
  24| cumulative.
  25| SECTION 21. FAMILY AND MEDICAL LEAVE
  26| The Town agrees to abide by the provisions of the 1993 Family and Medical Leave Act (FMLA). All eligible employees are
  27| entitled to up to twelve (12) weeks of unpaid leave for certain family and medical reasons in a one (1) year period.
  28| Employees are eligible for FMLA if they have worked for the Town for at least one (1) year and at least for one thousand two
  29| hundred and fifty (1,250) hours over the previous twelve (12) months.
  30| Unpaid FMLA leave must be granted for any of the following reasons:
  31|  To care for the employee’s child after birth, or placement for adoption or foster care
  32|  To care for the employee’s spouse, son, daughter, or parent, who has a serious health condition
  33|  For a serious health condition that makes the employee unable to perform the employee’s job
  34| In accordance with the past practice, the Town allows, at the option of the employee, the use of paid leave time during the
  35| FMLA absence including accumulated Sick Leave, Vacation Leave, and Personal Leave.  The Town requires all employees
  36| applying for an FMLA leave to notify their Department Head, in writing, thirty (30) days prior to the leave, if possible.
  37| SECTION 22.  BEREAVEMENT LEAVE
  38| In the event of death in an employee’s family (wife, husband, mother (in -law), father (in-law), child, grandparents, brother, or
  39| sister) up to and including three days off duty may be granted, with pay, not to be charged against sick leave.  Any time
  40| above the three days is to be charged against sick leave.
  41| SECTION 23.  MILITARY LEAVE
  42| Town employees with a minimum of six cons ecutive month’s employment with the Town who serve in the State or Federal
  43| military training forces under orders shall be allowed full pay for not more than one normal working week and shall be
  44| granted a military leave of absence for the period of their required service with said forces.
  45| Military leave of absence with pay shall be granted to employees of the Town on occasion of their appearance before local
  46| draft boards or draft appeal boards, or for physical examinations ordered by said boards; but no suc h leave shall be granted
  47| for a period of more than one day without the approval of the Personnel Committee.  Such leave shall be deemed to be a
  48| military leave of absence.
  49| A military leave of absence without pay shall be granted to any Town Officer or empl oyee called to active duty with the State
  50| or Federal Armed Forces for purposes other than the routine annual tour of duty for training purposes.

===PAGE 196=== (plain, 50 lines)
   1| 196
   2| As used herein, the phrases “military training forces” or “armed forces” shall mean the United States Air Forc e, the United
   3| States Army, the United States Navy, the Unites States Marine Corps, the United States Coast Guard, the Massachusetts
   4| National Guard, the Massachusetts Air National Guard, the Massachusetts State Guard, and any and all components thereof.
   5| SECTION 24.  JURY DUTY
   6| All persons permanently employed, whether full time or part time for a minimum of twenty (20) hours per week, called for
   7| jury duty shall be paid by the Town, an amount equal to the difference between the compensation paid for a normal working
   8| period and the amount paid by the court, excluding allowance for travel, and this will be certified by the Town Clerk or
   9| Treasurer upon presentation of the check for monies received for Jury Duty.
  10| SECTION 25.  LONGEVITY PAY
  11| After reaching ten (10) years of employment, and thereafter, each employee permanently employed, whether full or part time,
  12| shall receive an annual bonus equal to 2% of their annual salary, and is payable in the first pay period after July 1 st. At 15
  13| years of continuous service – 2.5% of their annual salary, and at 20 years of continuous service – 3% of their annual salary.
  14| SECTION 26.  TRAVEL REIMBURSEMENT
  15| Travel reimbursement shall be by signed voucher at a rate established by the IRS when using the employee’s own car, plus
  16| tolls and parking fees.
  17| SECTION 27.  DISCIPLINE POLICY
  18| Items which constitute grounds for disciplinary action against an employee include:  chronic absenteeism or tardiness,
  19| insubordination or refusal to work, infraction of work and safety rules, threat o f physical violence against fellow employees,
  20| use of profane language to a supervisor or fellow employee, or offenses of comparable gravity or severity.
  21| Upon determination by the Department Head that disciplinary action is warranted, the following guideli nes shall be
  22| implemented for all employees that have successfully completed a six (6) month probationary period:
  23| STEP 1. 1st Offense - Written warning.
  24| STEP 2. 2nd Offense - A formal written reprimand notifying the employee of a one day suspension and  that one further
  25| offense shall result in a three day suspension without pay.
  26| STEP 3. 3rd Offense - Employee will receive an immediate three day suspension without pay and a written notification that
  27| any further offense shall be grounds for dismissal.
  28| STEP 4. 4th Offense - Immediate dismissal of the employee.
  29| Depending on the particular circumstances, one or more of the above steps may be skipped. The offense for which
  30| disciplinary action is taken may be any combination of the items described above. For example , offense number one could be
  31| tardiness, offense number two could be absenteeism, and offense number three could be refusal to work or insubordination.
  32| If an employee goes twenty -four (24) months without a further offense, the first offense beyond twenty -four (24) months
  33| shall be disciplined at the level of step one.
  34| The following infractions are grounds for the immediate dismissal of an employee for just cause by the Department Head.
  35| Just cause may include, but shall not be limited to, the following:
  36|  Willful neglect or non-performance of one or more assigned duties;
  37|  Demonstrated incompetence in the performance of one or more assigned duties;
  38|  Behavior that seriously interferes with the normal operation of the municipality, the department, or any member o f the
  39| workforce:
  40|  Insubordination, which shall mean a refusal to carry out a direct order;
  41|  Dishonesty in the performance of assigned duties:
  42|  Chronic absenteeism or tardiness without reasonable excuse;
  43|  Unauthorized possession or use of alcohol or an unprescribed controlled substance during any period of assigned work;
  44|  Municipal theft.
  45| SECTION 28.  DEFINITIONS
  46| The following terms used in the Salary Administration Plan shall be defined as set forth herein:
  47| Full-Time Employee shall mean any person who spends an amount of time between thirty-six (36) and forty (40) hours of
  48| permanently scheduled work per week in the service of the Town.
  49| Hourly Employee shall mean any employee of the Town who by virtue of their classified position is compensated at an
  50| hourly rate for services rendered.  Hourly employees are not eligible for benefits (i.e. vacation, sick -time, insurance, etc.).

===PAGE 197=== (plain, 59 lines)
   1| 197
   2| Intermittent Employee shall mean any person who works less than twenty (20) hours per week on a regular basis.
   3| Intermittent employees are not eligible for benefits (i.e. vacation, sick-time, insurance, etc.) and work less than one thousand
   4| forty (1,040) hours per year in the service of the Town.
   5| Part Time Compensation Formula shall be determined by dividing the compensation or time off by 40 and then multiplying
   6| by the number of hours the employee works per week.   For example, a 40 hour per week employee is eligible for 120 hours
   7| of vacation.  If another employee with the same tenure works 36 hours, the formula is as follows:  120 / 40 = 3.  Then
   8| multiply 3 x 36 hours = 108 hours.
   9| Part-Time Employee shall mean any person who spends less than thirty-six (36) hours but twenty (20) hours or more of
  10| permanently scheduled work per week in the service of the Town.
  11| Probationary Period shall mean the initial six (6) month period of employment with the Town.
  12| Seasonal Employee shall mean any employee, part or full-time who spends no more than six (6) months or one hundred
  13| eighty (180) days whichever is less per year, in the service of the Town.  Seasonal employees are not eligible for benefits (i.e.
  14| vacation, sick-time, insurance, etc.).

   1| 199
   2| (Submitted by Personnel Committee) Board of Selectmen and Finance Committee recommends approval with amendments to
   3| be presented at Town Meeting. VOTED with amendment to replace all references in Section 1, first paragraph of Town
   4| Manager to Town Moderator.
   5| ARTICLE 14. To see if the Town will vote to raise and appropriate or transfer from available funds, a sum of money
   6| to the Stabilization Fund; or take any other action relative thereto. (Submitted by Town Manager) Board of Selectmen
   7| recommends approval of $6,623.00. Finance Committee recommends approval. VOTED UNANIMOUSLY to raise
   8| $7,136.00.
   9| ARTICLE 15. To see if the Town will vote to adopt a Non-Binding Resolution opposing the Northeast Energy Direct
  10| Project as printed below; or take any other action relative thereto. (Submitted by Board of Selectmen/ Town Manager)  Board
  11| of Selectmen recommends approval. Finance committee reports no direct financial impact.
  12| Non-Binding Resolution Opposing the Northeast Energy Direct Project
  13| WHEREAS, Kinder Morgan has proposed a new 36” high pressure gas pipeline through Massachusetts and New Hampshire,
  14| as part of a Northeast Energy Direct Project hereinafter called “the Project”; and
  15| WHEREAS, the Project also proposes a new 12” high pressure (1460 psi) natural gas pipeline, which it calls the “Fitchburg
  16| Lateral”, to be installed, not in Fitchburg, but in Lunenburg, Townsend, a nd Mason NH; and
  17| WHEREAS, Lunenburg is already crossed by a Kinder Morgan pipeline hereinafter called the “South Lunenburg Lateral”,
  18| which runs from Lancaster to the proposed southern terminus of the Fitchburg Lateral; and
  19| WHEREAS, the South Lunenburg Lateral has already caused financial damage to Lunenburg citizens; and
  20| WHEREAS, the completion of the Project appears to create a connection between the proposed Fitchburg Lateral and the
  21| South Lunenburg Lateral; and
  22| WHEREAS, said connection could provide Kinder Morgan the opportunity to provide gas service to Worcester through
  23| Lunenburg including a possible future addition of a compressor station in Lunenburg, or other re -adaptation and re-use of the
  24| South Lunenburg Lateral; and
  25| WHEREAS, Kinder Morgan has been silent about its plans to create such a connection, despite its proposing a metering
  26| station in Lunenburg at the Southern terminus of the proposed Fitchburg Lateral in its Resource Report 1 filed with the
  27| Federal Energy Regulatory Commission; and
  28| WHEREAS, the currently proposed pipeline route was chosen in a private, closed process so that there has been no chance
  29| for open, public discussion or debate of alternate routing possibilities and tradeoffs; and
  30| WHEREAS, Kinder Morgan identified properties in Lunenburg that it intends the Fitchburg Lateral to cross but declined to
  31| provide the town of Lunenburg with detailed and current information on the exact route it is proposing; and
  32| WHEREAS, landowners are being asked to permit surveying on their property or to sign agreements to forfeit some of their
  33| property rights; and
  34| WHEREAS, federal eminent domain powers may be used to forcibly take pipeline easements from unwilling landowners;
  35| and
  36| WHEREAS, a high-pressure gas pipeline, by its nature, carries the potential for leak, rupture or devastating explosion
  37| causing untold damage to property and lives; and
  38| WHEREAS, said pipeline goes against current Massachusetts commitments to renewable energies and combating global
  39| climate change; and
  40| WHEREAS, our energy challenges are better addressed through investment in energy conservation measures as well as green
  41| and renewable energy solutions; and
  42| WHEREAS, Lunenburg and our neighboring communities have adopted comprehensive master plans, zoning bylaws,
  43| wetlands bylaws and other land use controls to provide for the orderly development of our communities and the conservation
  44| and protection of our communities for future generations to come, as good stewards of the land should; and
  45| WHEREAS, the elimination of environmental threats to our forests and streams from improvident developments is the
  46| fundamental purpose for the adoption of our land use controls and master plans; and
  47| WHEREAS, Lunenburg and our neighboring communities have publicly and privately set aside large tracts of  land and
  48| restricted their development for conservation and open space purposes as part of their master plans;
  49| NOW, THEREFORE, be it RESOLVED that the Town of Lunenburg does hereby declare that it:
  50| STANDS IN OPPOSITION to Kinder Morgan’s proposed Energy Direct Project and to the Fitchburg Lateral; and
  51| STANDS IN OPPOSITION to any effort to locate the proposed Fitchburg Lateral within Lunenburg’s borders; and

===PAGE 200=== (plain, 9 lines)
   1| 200
   2| FURTHER COMMITS to participate in and provide comments to any meetings held under the Federal Energy Regulatory
   3| Commission (FERC) review process with regard to the proposed Fitchburg Lateral to oppose Kinder Morgan’s application;
   4| and
   5| BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Town of Lunenburg does hereby call upon its state and federal legislators and
   6| Executive branch officers to enact legislation and take any such other actions as are necessary to oppose such energy projects
   7| that go against our commitment to public safety, our environment, our economic well -being and our sense of community.
   8| VOTED
   9| Meeting adjourned at 9:26 P.M.
